On optimal p p -adic uniformization of unitary Shimura curves Thanks: Research of Haining Wang is partially supported by the National Natural Science Foundation of China (Grant number 12371009).
Michael Rapoport, Haining Wang

Abstract
. We prove variants of Cherednik’s theorem on p p -adic uniformization, for two classes of Shimura curves attached to unitary groups. The first class are PEL-type variants, defined in [ 23 ] and [ 24 ] , of the PEL-type Shimura curves of [ 20 ] . The advantage of these variants is that we can prove p p -adic uniformization in optimal form for these variants, for any level structure prime to the distinguished p p -adic place v 0 v_{0} of the totally real field F F (in [ 20 ] this optimal form was only proved for level structures which are prime to all p p -adic places). Here optimality refers to the fact that we define p p -integral models by extending the moduli problem over ℂ \mathbb{C} solved by the Shimura variety, and prove for them integral p p -adic uniformization in terms of the formal Drinfeld upper half plane Ω ^ F v 0 \widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}} for F v 0 F_{v_{0}} . This greater generality compared to [ 20 ] comes at the price of having to replace the reflex field by a bigger extension. The second class are Shimura curves attached to unitary groups. Here the construction of p p -integral models relies on the construction of canonical integral models in the sense of [ 21 ] . Again, the reflex field has to be replaced by a bigger extension–but this time due to our method of proof. We also identify the integral local Shimura curve for an anisotropic unitary group over ℚ p \mathbb{Q}_{p} .



1.
Introduction

The present paper is a sequel to
[
20
]
. Both papers establish analogues of Cherednik’s
p
-adic uniformization theorem for one-dimensional Shimura varieties which are, loosely speaking, attached to unitary groups.

Recall that Cherednik’s theorem is concerned with the Shimura curve associated to a quaternion algebra over a totally real field which satisfies specific hypotheses. More precisely, let
F
be a totally real number field. We fix an archimedean place
w_{0}
of
F
and a non-archimedean place
v_{0}
of residue characteristic
p
. Let
D
be a quaternion algebra over
F
which is split at
w_{0}
and non-split at all other archimedean places
w^{\prime}\neq w_{0}
, and which is non-split at
v_{0}
. We denote by
D^{\times}
the multiplicative group of
D
, but also the corresponding algebraic group over
F
or (by restriction of scalars) over
\mathbb{Q}
. There is a natural Shimura datum
X_{D^{\times}}
for
D^{\times}
, and an associated Shimura variety

(1.0.1)

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})=D^{\times}\backslash\big[\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}\times D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}\big].

Here
\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})\setminus\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the union of the upper and the lower halfplane, and the action of
D^{\times}
on
\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}
is via
D^{\times}D_{w_{0}}^{\times}
and an identification of
D_{w_{0}}^{\times}
with
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, which acts naturally on
\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}
.

The Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})
has a canonical model
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})_{F}
over the reflex field of
(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})
which can be identified with
F
and its complex embedding given by
w_{0}
. For Cherednik’s theorem we fix an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}
of the form

(1.0.2)

\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{v_{0}}\subset D^{\times}({\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{v_{0}}})\cdot D^{\times}(F_{v_{0}}),

where
\mathbf{K}_{v_{0}}
is the unique maximal compact subgroup of
D^{\times}_{v_{0}}
. Then Cherednik’s theorem asserts the existence of isomorphisms compatible with changes in
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}
,

(1.0.3)

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})_{F}\times_{\Spec F}\Spec\breve{F}_{v_{0}}\simeq{\bar{D}}^{\times}\backslash\big[(\Omega_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{{\mathrm{Sp}}\,F_{v_{0}}}{\mathrm{Sp}}\,\breve{F}_{v_{0}})\times D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}\big].

Here
\Omega_{F_{v_{0}}}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{F_{v_{0}}}\setminus\mathbb{P}^{1}(F_{v_{0}})
denotes the Drinfeld upper halfplane for the local field
F_{v_{0}}
, a rigid-analytic space over
F_{v_{0}}
. Also,
\bar{D}
denotes the quaternion algebra over
F
which is non-split at
w_{0}
, is split at
{v_{0}}
and locally coincides with
D
at all other places (the
castling
of
D
). The action of
\bar{D}^{\times}
on
\Omega_{F_{v_{0}}}
is via
\bar{D}^{\times}{\bar{D}}_{v_{0}}^{\times}
and an identification of
{\bar{D}}_{v_{0}}^{\times}
with
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
, which acts naturally on
\Omega_{F_{v_{0}}}
.
The isomorphism (
1.0.3
) is to be interpreted as follows: the rigid-analytic space on the RHS is (uniquely) algebraizable by the projective algebraic curve over
\breve{F}_{v_{0}}
on the left-hand side. Cherednik’s theorem thus asserts that one may pass from the tautological complex uniformization of the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})
to
p
-adic uniformization, provided that the level structure is prime to
v_{0}
.

We refer to the introduction of
[
20
]
for a discussion of the proof of Cherednik’s theorem and its generalizations. Here we only emphasize that when
F=\mathbb{Q}
, the Shimura curves
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})
are moduli spaces of abelian varieties with additional structure. Using this, Drinfeld
[
10
]
gave a moduli-theoretic proof of Cherednik’s theorem in this special case. Furthermore, he proved an ‘integral version’ of this theorem (which has the original version as a corollary). This integral version relies on a theorem on formal moduli spaces of
p
-divisible groups. When
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
, Cherednik’s Shimura curves do not represent a moduli problem of abelian varieties. In fact,
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(D^{\times},X_{D^{\times}})
is a Shimura variety of abelian type which is not of Hodge type. Furthermore, this Shimura variety has other bad properties: the weight cocharacter
w_{D^{\times}}
associated to
X_{D^{\times}}
is not defined over
\mathbb{Q}
and the split rank of the connected center
Z^{\circ}
over
\mathbb{Q}
and over
\mathbb{R}
differ, i.e.
\rank_{\mathbb{Q}}(Z^{\circ})\neq\rank_{\mathbb{R}}(Z^{\circ})
. Nonetheless, Boutot and Zink
[
4
]
have given a proof of Cherednik’s theorem by reduction to the case of PEL-Shimura varieties. They also compare the descent data down to
F_{v_{0}}
of both sides of (
1.0.3
). More precisely,
[
4
]
relates Cherednik’s Shimura varieties to Shimura varieties associated to certain
fake unitary groups
corresponding to central division algebras over a CM-field extension of
F
equipped with an involution of the second kind, for which
p
-adic uniformization is proved by Rapoport and Zink
[
25
]
. In
[
25
]
, these uniformization theorems appear as a special instance of a general
non-archimedean uniformization theorem
, which describes the formal completion of PEL-type Shimura varieties along a fixed isogeny class. In the case of
p
-adic uniformization, the whole special fiber forms a single isogeny class.

The paper
[
20
]
proves
p
-adic uniformization for Shimura curves attached to unitary similitude groups associated to (anti-)hermitian vector spaces
V
of dimension
2
over a CM-extension
K
of
F
, the KRZ Shimura curves. Analogously to Cherednik’s assumption on the quaternion algebra
D
, it is assumed in
[
20
]
that
V
is split at the archimedean
place
w_{0}
of
F
and ramified at all other archimedean places, and that
V
is ramified at the
p
-adic non-archimedean place
v_{0}
(in particular, it is assumed that
v_{0}
does
not
split in
K
). Of course, these Shimura curves are closely related to the Shimura curves considered by Cherednik (we refer to
[
19
]
for a general discussion of the relation between quaternion algebras and two-dimensional hermitian vector spaces). However, they are different. In particular, they represent a moduli problem of abelian varieties, their weight cocharacter is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\rank_{\mathbb{Q}}(Z^{\circ})=\rank_{\mathbb{R}}(Z^{\circ})
. The downside of these Shimura varieties compared to those of Cherednik is that the reflex field
F
of Cherednik has to be replaced by a bigger reflex field
E
. The uniformization theorem of
[
20
]
is optimal when the level structure imposed is prime to
p
, in the sense that it extends to an integral uniformization that allows an explicit interpretation of the points in the reduction modulo
p
. In particular, the uniformization theorem of
[
20
]
is optimal when
v_{0}
is the only place of
F
over
p
because then the conditions that the level structure be prime to
p
coincides with Cherednik’s condition that the level structure be prime to
v_{0}
.

The first aim of the present paper is to prove an
optimal uniformization theorem
when there are more places over
p
, i.e., when there are
banal

p
-adic places, in the terminology of
[
20
, §7]
. An approximation to such an optimal theorem is proved in
[
20
, §7]
, but this is not completely satisfactory (as pointed out in the introduction of
[
20
]
). Here we succeed in proving such an optimal theorem by replacing the Shimura variety of
[
20
]
by the variant introduced in
[
23
]
,
[
24
]
, the RSZ Shimura curves. The downside of this variant is that its reflex field is (even) bigger than
E
.

The second aim of the present paper is to establish
p
-adic uniformization theorems for Shimura curves attached to unitary groups, under the same local assumptions. These Shimura curves are not of PEL type but, assuming that canonical
p
-integral models in the sense of
[
21
]
exist, we construct
p
-integral models over
O_{K}
of these Shimura curves. The optimal version of
p
-adic uniformization for them should hold over
K
. Unfortunately, we cannot prove this optimal version when
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
but rather have to make a base change to a finite extension of
K
. It seems a worthwhile goal to remove this extension of the reflex field. As a reward, we expect interesting arithmetic applications (level raising, level lowering, bounding Selmer groups), cf.
[
19
, Introduction]
. We also give an explicit model of the
integral local Shimura variety
, in the sense of Scholze-Weinstein
[
27
]
(comp. also
[
22
]
) attached to an anisotropic unitary group over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, again at least after an extension of the reflex field.

We now summarize the layout of this article. The paper consists of four parts. In the first part, we state our main results and give some background. More precisely, in §
2
, we recall the definition of the Shimura curve in
[
23
]
,
[
24
]
which we will refer to as the RSZ Shimura curve. We formulate the associated moduli problem and introduce the terminology used throughout the paper. We also give the precise formulation of our main results. In section
3
, we review the notions of CM-types and CM-triples from earlier papers
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
. In section
4
, we review the main theorem of complex multiplication for CM abelian varieties and its local analogue, the precise description of the Rapoport-Zink space associated to a torus with its Weil descent datum. The proofs in the latter theory are based on the
contraction functor
of
[
20
]
(in the case of rank one, which is more elementary than the case of rank two treated in
[
20
]
). The notion of the
special element
relative to
(\Phi_{0}^{+},E)
(Construction
4.2.1
) is used throughout the paper.

The second part is devoted to the proof of our first main theorem which concerns the RSZ Shimura curves. In section
5
, we treat the local theory. Here the case of special CM-triples is handled by citing rather directly from
[
20
]
. However, the case of banal CM-triples requires going back to the proofs in
[
20
]
. Indeed, in
[
20
]
, the descent datum in the banal case is not explicit. It is here that the advantage of the RSZ Shimura curves over the KRZ Shimura curves becomes apparent since in the RSZ case we are able to give the descent datum explicitly. In section
6
, we prove the first main theorem, as a rather direct consequence of the local case.

In the third part we go back to
[
20
]
and explain that the deficiencies of KRZ Shimura curves go away when extending slightly the reflex field, replacing it by an extension of degree
\leq 2
. After making this extension, we can make the descent datum completely explicit (but still the level of the Shimura curve has to be prime to
p
, like in
[
20
]
). This is the subject matter of §
7
. In §
8
, we compare KRZ Shimura curves with RSZ Shimura curves.

The fourth part is devoted to the proof of the second main theorem which concerns the Shimura curves attached to unitary groups. In §
9
, we treat the local theory, by reduction to the case of the Rapoport-Zink space of RSZ CM-triples considered in §
5
and the case of the Rapoport-Zink space of a torus considered in §
4
. Here we use the theory of integral local Shimura varieties from
[
22
]
. In §
10
, we treat the global case which works in complete parallel to the local case, except that we are basing ourselves on the conjectural theory of global canonical integral models of
[
21
]
. In our context, this theory is established when
p\neq 2
and the special place
v_{0}
is unramified
[
7
]
; the case of a ramified special place seems within reach. Since our approach is axiomatic, this possible deficiency of the literature plays no role in our exposition.

1.1.
Notation

We use the following notation and conventions without comment in this paper.

•

If
M
is a number field, we write
\mathbb{A}_{M}
for its adèle ring and denote by
\mathbb{A}_{M,f}
its finite part and by
\mathbb{A}^{S}_{M,f}
its prime to
S
finite part for a finite collection of finite places
S
of
M
. Let
p
be a prime, and
v_{0}
be a place of
M
above
p
. We use the notation
M_{p}=\prod_{v\mid p}M_{v}
and
M^{v_{0}}_{p}=\prod_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}M_{v}
.

•

Let
F
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
K/F
be an étale algebra of rank
2
, with corresponding involution
a\bar{a}
. Let
V
be a free
K
-module, equipped with an alternating
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-bilinear form
\psi\colon V\times V\mathbb{Q}_{p}
such that
\psi(av,v^{\prime})=\psi(v,\bar{a}v^{\prime})
for
a\in K
and
v,v^{\prime}\in V
. Let
\Lambda
be a
O_{K}
-lattice in
V
. Then the dual
O_{K}
-lattice is
\Lambda^{\vee}=\{x\in V\mid\psi(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{ for all }y\in\Lambda\}
. The lattice
\Lambda
is called
almost self-dual
if
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\vee}
if
K/F
is a ramified field extension or an unramified field extension and the anti-hermitian form associated to
\psi
is split or if
K/F
is split; if
K/F
is an unramified field extension and the anti-hermitian form associated to
\psi
is non-split we ask that
\Lambda
is contained in
\Lambda^{\vee}
with colength one.

•

If
O
is a discrete valuation ring, we write
{\rm Nilp}_{O}
for the category of
O
-algebras
R
such that the image of a uniformizer
\pi
in
R
is nilpotent. Similarly, we denote by
({\rm Sch}/\Spf O)
the category of
O
-schemes such that
\pi\mathcal{O}_{S}
is a locally nilpotent ideal sheaf. Let
k
be the residue field of
O
. If
S
is a scheme over
O
, we denote by
\bar{S}
the special fiber of
S
, i.e.,
\bar{S}=S\times_{\Spec O}\Spec k
.

•

Let
O
be a ring, then
{\rm(LNSch)}/O
denotes the category of locally noetherian schemes over
O
.

Part I
Main results and preliminaries

2.
Statement of the main results

2.1.
The RSZ Shimura variety

Let us first recall the Shimura curve in
[
20
]
. Let
K
be a CM-field, with totally real subfield
F
. We denote the non-trivial
F
-automorphism of
K
by
a\bar{a}
. Let
V
be a two-dimensional
K
-vector space, equipped with an alternating
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear form

(2.1.1)

\varsigma\colon V\times V\mathbb{Q}

such that

(2.1.2)

\varsigma(ax,y)=\varsigma(x,\bar{a}y),\quad x,y\in V,\,a\in K.

We call
\varsigma
a
anti-hermitian alternating form
(w.r.t.
K/F
). There is a unique anti-hermitian form
\varkappa
on
V
such that

(2.1.3)

\Trace_{K/\mathbb{Q}}a\varkappa(x,y)=\varsigma(ax,y),\quad x,y\in V,\,a\in K.

Conversely, the anti-hermitian form
\varkappa
determines the alternating bilinear form
\varsigma
with (
2.1.2
). We say that
\varkappa
arises from
\varsigma
by contraction.

Recall that anti-hermitian spaces
V
are determined up to isomorphism by their signature at the archimedean places of
F
and their local invariants
{\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(V)\in\{\pm 1\}
at the non-archimedean places
v
of
F
. Let
w_{0}
be an archimedean place such that
V_{w_{0}}
has signature
(1,1)
and such that
V_{w}
is definite for all archimedean places
w\neq w_{0}
. Let us be more precise. Let
\Phi=\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}$-Alg}}(K,\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Let
r
be a
generalized CM-type of rank 2, special with respect to
w_{0}
, i.e., a function

(2.1.4)

r:\Phi\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},\qquad\,\,\varphi r_{\varphi},

such that
r_{\varphi}+r_{\bar{\varphi}}=2
for all
\varphi\in\Phi
, and such that for the extensions
\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}
of
w_{0}
we have
r_{\varphi_{0}}=r_{\bar{\varphi}_{0}}=1
and with
r_{\varphi}\in\{0,2\}
for
\varphi\notin\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}
, comp.
[
19
]
. Then we demand that the signature of
V_{\varphi}=V\otimes_{K,\varphi}\mathbb{C}
be equal to
(r_{\varphi},2-r_{\varphi})
.

Remark 2.1.1
.

Note that, because we are in very small rank, the generalized CM-type
\Phi
is of
fake Drinfeld type
relative to
\varphi_{0}
and relative to
\bar{\varphi}_{0}
, in the sense of
[
24
, Ex. 2.3, (i)]
.

We denote the reflex field of
r
by
E_{r}
. It is a subfield of
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
, the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{C}
. Note that
F
embeds via
\varphi_{0}
(or
\bar{\varphi}_{0}
) into
E_{r}
, and that the archimedean place of
F
induced by

(2.1.5)

F\overset{\varphi_{0}}{\longrightarrow}E_{r}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

is equal to
w_{0}
. If
F=\mathbb{Q}
, then
E_{r}=F
.

Associated to these data, there is a Shimura pair
(G,X_{G})
. Here
G
denotes the group of unitary
similitudes of
V
, with similitude factor in
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, an algebraic subgroup of
{\rm GSp}(V,\psi)
over
\mathbb{Q}
. The corresponding Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}}(G,X_{G})
is the one appearing in
[
20
]
. We also refer to this Shimura variety as the KRZ Shimura curve.

Now we are going to define the Shimura variety of
[
24
]
, the RSZ
Shimura curve
. We fix a complex embedding
\varphi_{0}\in\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}
. We also fix a
classical
CM-type, i.e., a half-system of complex embeddings
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}\subset\Phi
. We make the assumption

\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
.

An example of
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
is given by

(2.1.6)

\quad\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}\setminus\{\varphi_{0}\}=\{\varphi\in\Phi\mid r_{\varphi}=2\}.

This classical CM-type
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}=\Phi^{+}_{\varphi_{0},r}
is called the
CM-type canonically associated to
r
and
\varphi_{0}
. Then
r
is the
strict fake Drinfeld generalized CM-type associated to

(\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}},\varphi_{0})
, in the terminology of
[
24
, Ex. 2.3, (ii)]
.

Let

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}=\{z\in\Res_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\mid\Nm_{K/F}(z)\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\}.

The choice of
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
defines a Shimura datum
(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
with reflex field
E_{\Phi^{+}}
, where
X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
is the diagonal homomorphism

h_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\colon\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}=\prod\nolimits_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})..

We therefore obtain a Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
. This Shimura variety will only play an auxiliary role, and we will take for
\mathbf{K}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
the unique maximal compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\circ}
of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,

(2.1.7)

\mathbf{K}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\circ}=\{z\in(O_{K}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\mid\Nm_{K/F}(z)\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}\}.

The Shimura variety in which we are really interested is associated to the fiber product group

(2.1.8)

\widetilde{G}=G\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}},

where the maps to
\mathbb{G}_{m}
are given by
\Nm_{K/F}
, resp. the similitude character of
G
. The second part
X_{\widetilde{G}}
of the Shimura datum is given by the conjugacy class of homomorphisms,

h_{\widetilde{G}}\colon\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{(h_{G},h_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})}\widetilde{G}_{\mathbb{R}}.

For an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, there is a Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
,
whose complex points are given by

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})(\mathbb{C})\simeq\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}].

Here
\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}
is acted on by

\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{R})
via the projection to
{\rm GU}(V_{w_{0}})_{\rm ad}
and a fixed
isomorphism

{\rm GU}(V_{w_{0}})_{\rm ad}(\mathbb{R})\simeq{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

The reflex field
E
of
(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
is the composite
E=E_{r,\Phi^{+}}=E_{r}E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}
, cf.
[
24
, (3.4)]
. In the case when
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}=\Phi_{\varphi_{0},r}
is the canonical CM-type associated to
\varphi_{0},r
, it follows from (
2.1.6
) that
E=\varphi_{0}(K)E_{r}
, and is either
E_{r}
or a quadratic extension of
E_{r}
. The Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
has a canonical model
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})_{E}
over
E
.

The torus
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
embeds naturally as a central subgroup of
G
, which gives rise to a product decomposition

(2.1.9)

\widetilde{G}\simeq\mathrm{U}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}},

where
\mathrm{U}\subset G
is the kernel of the multiplier map, cf.
[
24
, (3.5)]
. Here
\mathrm{U}
is considered as algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. We will only consider compact open subgroups
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which, in terms of the product decomposition (
2.1.9
) are of the form

(2.1.10)

\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}},

where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

2.2.
The RSZ moduli problem over
E

The Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
is of PEL-type. We will now formulate the corresponding moduli problem. We will first define a moduli problem for
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
, cf.
[
24
, §3]
. For economy of exposition, we will actually define this moduli problem over
\Spec O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
.

We fix an auxiliary non-zero ideal
\mathfrak{a}
of
O_{K}
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
be the category fibered in groupoids over
{\rm(LNSch)}/O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
which associates to each
O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
-scheme
S
the groupoid of triples
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
, where

(1)

A_{0}
is an abelian scheme over
S
;

(2)

\iota_{0}\colon O_{K}\End(A_{0})
is an action of
O_{K}
on
A_{0}
;

(3)

\lambda_{0}
is a polarization on
A_{0}
.

We impose the conditions that
\ker\lambda_{0}=A_{0}[\mathfrak{a}]
and that for the Rosati involution of
\lambda_{0}
,

(2.2.1)

{\rm Ros}_{\lambda_{0}}\bigl(\iota_{0}(a)\bigr)=\iota_{0}(\bar{a})\quad\text{for all}\quad a\in O_{K},

and that
A_{0}
is of CM-type
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
, i.e.

(2.2.2)

\charac\bigl(\iota_{0}(a)\mid\Lie A_{0}\bigr)=\prod\nolimits_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}(T-\varphi(a))\quad\text{for all}\quad a\in O_{K}.

Here the left-hand side in (
2.2.2
) denotes the characteristic polynomial of the action of
\iota_{0}(a)
on the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\Lie A_{0}
; the right-hand side, which is a priori a polynomial with coefficients in
O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
, is regarded as an element of
\mathcal{O}_{S}[T]
via the structure morphism.
The morphisms
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})(A_{0}^{\prime},\iota_{0}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
in this groupoid are the
O_{K}
-linear isomorphisms
\phi_{0}\colon A_{0}A_{0}^{\prime}
such that the pullback of
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
is
\lambda_{0}
.

This moduli problem is representable by a Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
which is finite and étale over
\Spec O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
, cf.
[
24
, §3.4]
. For suitable choice of
\mathfrak{a}
, the stack
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
is non-empty. We fix such a choice of
\mathfrak{a}
in the sequel, which we assume to be prime to the prime number
p
to be fixed later, and write
\mathcal{A}_{0}
for
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
.

The moduli stack
\mathcal{A}_{0}\otimes_{O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}}\mathbb{C}
is a disjoint sum of copies of the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
. In order to single out one copy, we fix a locally free
O_{K}
-module
\Lambda_{0}
of rank one equipped with a nondegenerate alternating form
\langle\text{~,~}\rangle_{0}\colon\Lambda_{0}\times\Lambda_{0}\mathbb{Z}
such that
\langle ax,y\rangle_{0}=\langle x,\bar{a}y\rangle_{0}
for all
x,y\in\Lambda_{0}
and
a\in O_{K}
, such that the dual lattice
\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}
of
\Lambda_{0}
inside
\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
equals
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\Lambda_{0}
, and such that the anti-hermitian form on
\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{O_{K},\varphi}\mathbb{C}
obtained by contraction from
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{0}
is of signature
(1,0)
for every
\varphi\in\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
. We denote by
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
the open and closed substack of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
such that

\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\mathbb{C})=\{(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathrm{H}_{1}(A_{0},\mathbb{Z})\sim\Lambda_{0}\}.

Here the
O_{K}
-module
\mathrm{H}_{1}(A_{0},\mathbb{Z})
is equipped with the natural
\mathbb{Z}
-valued Riemann form induced by the polarization, and the
\sim
-sign means that
\mathrm{H}_{1}(A_{0},\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}
and
\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}
are
\widehat{O}_{K}
-linearly similar up to a factor in
\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}
and that
\mathrm{H}_{1}(A_{0},\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
and
\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
are
K
-linearly similar up to a (necessarily positive) factor in
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
. Then there is an identification

(2.2.3)

\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\mathbb{C})={\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})(\mathbb{C}),

cf.
[
24
, (3.15)]
.

Now we can formulate a moduli problem for the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
, i.e., a functor over
{\rm(LNSch)}/E
. We introduce the
K/F
-hermitian
space of dimension
2
,

(2.2.4)

\widetilde{V}=\Hom_{K}(V_{0},V),

where
V_{0}=\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, with its anti-hermitian form obtained by contraction from
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{0}
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

Let
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
be the category fibered in groupoids over
{\rm(LNSch)}/E
which associates to each
E
-scheme
S
the groupoid of tuples
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta})
, where

(1)

(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
is an object of
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}(S)
;

(2)

A
is an abelian scheme over
S
;

(3)

\iota\colon K\End^{0}(A)
is an action of
K
on
A
up to isogeny satisfying the Kottwitz condition
(\mathrm{KR}_{r})
:

(2.2.5)

\charac\bigl(\iota(a)\mid\Lie A\bigr)=\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi}(T-\varphi(a))^{r_{\varphi}}\quad\text{for all}\quad a\in K;

(4)

\lambda
is a quasi-polarization on
A
whose Rosati involution satisfies

{\rm Ros}_{\lambda}\bigl(\iota(a)\bigr)=\iota(\bar{a})
for
a\in K
;

(5)

\bar{\eta}
is a
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
-orbit (equivalently, a
{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
-orbit, where
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
acts through its projection
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
) of isometries of
\mathbb{A}_{K,f}/\mathbb{A}_{F,f}
-hermitian modules

(2.2.6)

\eta\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0},A)\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}.

Here we define

(2.2.7)

\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0},A):=\Hom_{\mathbb{A}_{K,f}}\bigl(\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0}),\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A)\bigr),

where
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0})
and
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A)
are the product of rational Tate modules of
A_{0}
and
A
. Here
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0},A)
is endowed with its natural
\mathbb{A}_{K,f}
-valued hermitian form
h
,

(2.2.8)

h(x,y):=\lambda_{0}^{-1}\circ y^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ x\in\End_{\mathbb{A}_{K,f}}\bigl(\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0})\bigr)=\mathbb{A}_{K,f},\quad x,y\in\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0},A),

cf.
[
24
, §3]
, where
y^{\vee}\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A^{\vee})\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0}^{\vee})
denotes the adjoint of
y
with respect to the Weil pairings on
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A)\times\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A^{\vee})
and
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0})\times\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0}^{\vee})
. Furthermore, for any geometric point
\bar{s}S
, the orbit
\bar{\eta}
is required to be
\pi_{1}(S,\bar{s})
-stable with respect to the
\pi_{1}(S,\bar{s})
-action on the fiber

\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0},A)(\bar{s})=\Hom_{\mathbb{A}_{K,f}}(\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{0,\bar{s}}),\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A_{\bar{s}})).

A morphism
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta})(A^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime})
in this groupoid is given by an isomorphism
\mu_{0}\colon(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(S)
and a
K
-linear quasi-isogeny
\phi\colon AA^{\prime}
pulling
\lambda^{\prime}
back to
\lambda
and
\bar{\eta}^{\prime}
back to
\bar{\eta}
.

Proposition 2.2.2
.

The moduli problem
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
is representable by a Deligne-Mumford stack
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
over
\Spec E
, and

\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}(\mathbb{C})={\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}}),

compatible with changing
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
of the form (
2.1.10
). If
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
is small enough, then
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
is a scheme.

Proof.

The representability statement is standard, and the identification of the
\mathbb{C}
-points is
[
24
, Theorem 3.7]
(the main point being the Hasse principle for hermitian spaces). For the last statement, we have to show that for a point
x=(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta})
of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
, the automorphism group of
x
is trivial.
However, an element of this automorphism group defines an element in an arithmetic subgroup of a unitary group which is compact at all archimedean places of
F
. It is therefore of finite order. By making
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
small enough, its eigenvalues are forced to be congruent to
1
modulo some big enough integer, hence are equal to
1
. The assertion follows.
∎

2.3.
The
p
-integral RSZ moduli problem

Let
p
be a prime number and fix an embedding
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
v_{0}
be a
p
-adic place of
F
which is non-split in
K
and such that
V_{v_{0}}
is a non-split
K_{v_{0}}/F_{v_{0}}
-anti-hermitian space, i.e.,
{\mathrm{inv}}_{v_{0}}(V)=-1
. Throughout the paper, we always assume
1
1

1

In light of the results of Kirch
[
13
]
, it should be possible to remove this blanket assumption.

p\neq 2
if
v_{0}
is ramified in
K
. Let
\nu
be the place of
E
induced by the embedding
\nu:\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We assume that the place
v_{0}
is induced by
\nu
under the inclusion
F\hookrightarrow E
given by
\varphi_{0}
. In the sequel, we consider
F_{v_{0}}
and
K_{v_{0}}
as subfields of
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(recall that
\varphi_{0}:K\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
is fixed).

Let us write
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\mid p}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where we denote by
\mathrm{U}_{v}={\rm Res}_{F_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{U}(V_{v}))
the corresponding unitary group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
for
V_{v}
. We define
\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}):=\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
where
\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We define
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

In the product decomposition

\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}},

we fix
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
and vary the open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which we write in the form

{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}},

where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
and
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We impose that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}
further decomposes as
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}\cdot\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}
, where
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We assume that
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
is a maximal open compact and therefore
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}=\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, as follows from our assumptions that
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a compact group. To simplify the formulation of the moduli problem, we assume that

\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}\subset\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v}^{\circ},

where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v}^{\circ}
is the stabilizer of a fixed lattice
\Lambda_{v}
in
V_{v}
which is almost selfdual (see the Notation section at the end of the Introduction for the meaning of this terminology). Let
{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}={\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G}}={\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

Remark 2.3.1
.

The subgroup
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
is a parahoric in
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
when
K_{v_{0}}/F_{v_{0}}
is unramified; it contains a parahoric with index
2
when
K_{v_{0}}/F_{v_{0}}
is ramified.

We are now going to describe a model
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
over
\Spec O_{E,(\nu)}
by formulating a moduli problem over
({\rm LNSch})/O_{E,(\nu)}
.

Definition 2.3.2
.

Let
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
be the category fibered in groupoids over
{\rm(LNSch)}/O_{E,(\nu)}
which associates to each
O_{E,(\nu)}
-scheme
S
the groupoid of tuples
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{p},\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}})
, where

(1)

(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
is an object of
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}(S)
;

(2)

A
is an abelian scheme over
S
;

(3)

\iota\colon O_{K,(p)}\End_{(p)}(A)=\End(A)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
is an action up to prime-to-
p
isogeny on
A
satisfying the Kottwitz condition (
2.2.5
) on
O_{K,(p)}
;

(4)

\lambda\in\Hom_{(p)}(A,A^{\vee})
is a quasi-polarization on
A
whose Rosati involution satisfies

{\rm Ros}_{\lambda}\bigl(\iota(a)\bigr)=\iota(\bar{a})
for
a\in O_{K,(p)}
;

(5)

\bar{\eta}^{p}
is a
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}
-orbit of isometries of
\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p}/\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{p}
-hermitian modules

(2.3.1)

\eta^{p}\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A_{0},A)\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p},

where
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A_{0},A)=\Hom_{\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p}}(\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A_{0}),\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A))
(as usual, a Galois-invariance property is imposed, comp. Definition
2.2.1
);

(6)

\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}}
is a
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}
-orbit of isometries of
K_{p}^{v_{0}}/F_{p}^{v_{0}}
-hermitian modules

(2.3.2)

\eta_{p}^{v_{0}}\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}_{p}^{v_{0}}(A_{0},A)\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}K_{p}^{v_{0}}.

Here the notation is as follows. Under the decomposition of
p
-divisible groups induced by the action of
O_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cong\prod_{v\mid p}O_{F,v}
, we have

A_{0}[p^{\infty}]=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}A_{0}[v^{\infty}],\quad\text{resp.}\quad A[p^{\infty}]=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}A[v^{\infty}].

We set

(2.3.3)

A_{0}[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}}=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}A_{0}[v^{\infty}],\quad\text{resp.}\quad A[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}}=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}A[v^{\infty}].

Furthermore,

\widehat{}\mathrm{V}_{p}^{v_{0}}(A_{0},A):=\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K_{p}^{v_{0}}}}(A_{0}[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}},A[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}})\otimes_{O_{K_{p}^{v_{0}}}}K_{p}^{v_{0}}

is the
p
-adic étale sheaf of
[
20
, Definition 7.4.1]
. More precisely, (
2.3.2
)
is an equivalence class of
isogenies of (pro-)étale sheaves

\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K_{p}^{v_{0}}}}(A_{0}[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}},A[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}})\mathcal{L},

where
\mathcal{L}\subset\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}K_{p}^{v_{0}}
is a variable
lattice. We refer to the datum (
2.3.2
) as a
CL-level structure on
A
.

We impose the following further conditions on the above tuples:

(i)

The action of
O_{K,(p)}
on
\Lie A
satisfies the Eisenstein condition
({\rm EC}_{r})
relative to
r
, cf.
[
20
, §2.2]
;

(ii)

For every
v\mid p
, the polarization of
p
-divisible groups induced by
\lambda

\lambda_{v}\colon A[v^{\infty}]A^{\vee}[v^{\infty}]\cong A[v^{\infty}]^{\vee}

is principal, except when
v
is unramified and
{\mathrm{inv}}(V_{v})=-1
, in which case
\ker\lambda_{v}
is contained in
A[\pi_{v}^{\infty}][\pi_{v}]
of rank
\#(\Lambda_{v}^{\vee}/\Lambda_{v})
;

(iii)

We require that at every geometric point
\bar{s}
of
S
the following
sign condition
holds for every non-split place
v\mid p
,

(2.3.4)

{\mathrm{inv}}^{r}_{v}(A_{0,\bar{s}},\iota_{0,\bar{s}},\lambda_{0,\bar{s}},A_{\bar{s}},\iota_{\bar{s}},\lambda_{\bar{s}})={\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(\widetilde{V}),

where the right-hand denotes the Hasse invariant of the hermitian space
\widetilde{V}
at
v
. Here the left-hand side is the sign from
[
23
, App. A]
. To make the connection with the definition of
[
23
, §3]
, we point out that
{\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(V)={\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(\widetilde{V})
.

A morphism
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{p},\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}})(A_{0}^{\prime},\iota_{0}^{\prime},\lambda_{0}^{\prime},A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime p},\bar{\eta}_{p}^{\prime v_{0}})
in this groupoid is given by an isomorphism
\phi_{0}\colon(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})(A_{0}^{\prime},\iota_{0}^{\prime},\lambda_{0}^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}(S)
and an
O_{K,(p)}
-linear quasi-isogeny
\phi\colon AA^{\prime}
inducing an isomorphism
A[p^{\infty}]A^{\prime}[p^{\infty}]
, pulling
\lambda^{\prime}
back to
\lambda
, and pulling
\bar{\eta}^{\prime p}
back to
\bar{\eta}^{p}
and pulling
\bar{\eta}_{p}^{\prime v_{0}}
back to
\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}}
.

Let
(\bar{V},\bar{\varkappa})
be the nearby anti-hermitian space relative to
w_{0}
and
v_{0}
. This means
\bar{V}
is isomorphic to
V
at all places except for
w_{0}
and
v_{0}
and such that it has signature
(2,0)
at
w_{0}
and
{\mathrm{inv}}_{v_{0}}(\bar{V})=+1
. Let
J_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathrm{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{U}(\bar{V})
and let
J={G}(\bar{V},\bar{\varkappa})
, the unitary similitude group of
(\bar{V},\bar{\varkappa})
with rational similitude factor. Then we define

(2.3.5)

\widetilde{J}=J_{\mathrm{U}}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}=J\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}.

Then
\mathrm{U}(\bar{V})
is anisotropic at the archimedean place
w_{0}
and quasi-split at
v_{0}
, and locally coincides
with
\mathrm{U}(V)
at all places
\neq v_{0},w_{0}
of
F
. Then
\widetilde{J}
is an inner form of
\widetilde{G}
. We write

J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}J_{\mathrm{U},v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\mathrm{U}(\bar{V}_{v})(F_{v}).

We fix an identification of
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
.

Now we can formulate the first main theorem of this paper.

Theorem 2.3.3
.

Assume that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}
is small enough.

(i)

The functor
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
is representable by a projective flat
O_{E,(\nu)}
-scheme
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
over
\Spec O_{E,(\nu)}
. Its generic fiber is identified with
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
, which is the canonical model of the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
.

(ii)

Let
(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}
be the formal completion of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
along its special fiber, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}
. Then there exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
,

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{{\rm Spf}\,O_{E_{\nu}}}{\rm Spf}\,O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{{\rm Spf}\,O_{F_{v_{0}}}}{\rm Spf}\,O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big]\,.

The quotient on the right-hand side is defined using the composition of maps

\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

and an identification of the adjoint group
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
and through an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}
. For varying
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G}}
, this isomorphism is compatible with the action of
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
through Hecke correspondences on both sides.

Furthermore, the natural descent datum down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on the left-hand side is given on the right-hand side by

(\xi,h)\rightarrow(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}h),\quad h\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

Here
\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}
is the natural descent datum down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on the first factor and
\widetilde{w}={\mathfrak{r}}_{\Phi^{+},E_{\nu}}(\varpi_{\nu})
is the
special element
in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which is considered as an element in
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(special relative to
(\Phi^{+},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
, as in Construction
6.1.1
).

Remark 2.3.4
.

As in
[
20
, Theorem 7.3.3]
, one can characterize the relative curve
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
over
O_{E,(\nu)}
as the unique stable relative curve in the sense of Deligne-Mumford with generic fiber
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
.

From Theorem
2.3.3
we obtain the analogue of Cherednik’s
p
-adic uniformization theorem in the generic fiber.

Corollary 2.3.5
.

There is an
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-equivariant isomorphism of rigid analytic spaces

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}\times_{\Spec{E}}\Spec{\breve{E}}_{\nu})^{\rm rig}\simeq\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big({\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{{\rm Sp}\,{F_{v_{0}}}}{{\rm Sp}\,{\breve{E}}_{\nu}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big]\,

over
{\rm Sp}\,{\breve{E}}_{\nu}
with compatible descent data. Here the left-hand side is equipped with the natural descent datum down to
E_{\nu}
and on the right-hand side the descent datum is given by

(\xi,h)\rightarrow(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}h),\quad h\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),

where
\widetilde{w}
is the special element in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which is considered as an element in
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(special relative to
(\Phi^{+},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
,as in Construction
6.1.1
).

Remark 2.3.6
.

We therefore see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
has
p
-adic uniformization at all places
\nu
of
E
over
v_{0}
. This is quite remarkable, when compared to the
p
-adic uniformization theorem for fake unitary groups of
[
25
]
. Indeed, to simplify let us take
F=\mathbb{Q}
in
loc. cit.
. Then for
n\geq 3
, the reflex field
E
is a quadratic extension of
\mathbb{Q}
in which
p
is split. At one of the two places of
E
above
p
, the Shimura variety has
p
-adic uniformization; at the other place the Shimura variety does not have
p
-adic uniformization (the special fiber contains more than one isogeny class).

As usual, this global uniformization theorem is a consequence of a corresponding local result, which is the conjunction of Proposition
5.1.6
and
5.2.4
.

2.4.
The Shimura variety for the unitary group

Our second main result concerns the Shimura curve associated to the unitary group
\mathrm{U}
and the Shimura datum
{X}_{\mathrm{U}}
given by the conjugacy class of homomorphisms

(2.4.1)

h_{\mathrm{U}}\colon\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow\mathrm{U}_{\mathbb{R}}

which we now define. Fix a CM type
\Phi^{+}
for
K/F
with
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{+}
. We choose for each
\varphi\in\Phi^{+}
a
\mathbb{C}
-basis of
V\otimes_{K,\varphi}\mathbb{C}
such that the anti-hermitian form is given by
\diag(i\cdot 1_{r_{\varphi}},(-i)\cdot 1_{r_{\bar{\varphi}}})
. Then we can write

\mathrm{U}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}=\prod\nolimits_{\Phi^{+}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}/\mathbb{C}.

Correspondingly
h_{\mathrm{U}}=(h_{\varphi})_{\varphi\in\Phi^{+}}
. We set

(2.4.2)

h_{\varphi}(z)=\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \diag(1,z/\bar{z}),&\quad\varphi=\varphi_{0}\\
1,&\quad\varphi\neq\varphi_{0}.\\
\end{aligned}\end{cases}

The associated reflex field is
E(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})=K
(embedded via
\varphi_{0}
in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
).
For an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, there is a Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
over
K
,
whose complex points are given by

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}].

Note that this Shimura variety is of abelian type but not of PEL type.

We continue to assume that the place
v_{0}
of
F
remains prime in
K
and that the anti-hermitian space
V_{v_{0}}
is non-split. Assume that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
is of the form
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
, where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}=\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(recall that, by our assumption, this is a compact group), where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}=\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v}\subset\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a product of parahoric stabilizers of the fixed almost selfdual lattice
\Lambda_{v}
, and where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is a sufficiently small open compact subgroup. We consider the
canonical integral model

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
of this Shimura variety of abelian type over
O_{K_{v_{0}}}
, in the sense of
[
21
]
.

Remark 2.4.1
.

The canonical integral model is known to exist when
v_{0}
is unramified and
p\neq 2
, cf.
[
7
]
. When
v_{0}
is ramified or
p=2
, we will assume the existence of the canonical integral model. We refer to Remark
10.1.2
for a more thorough discussion.

We introduce the following finite extension of
K
contained in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
,

(2.4.3)

E(\varphi_{0})=\bigcap\nolimits_{\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{\prime,+}}E_{\Phi^{\prime,+}}K,

(intersection of the join with
K
of the reflex fields of all CM-types
\Phi^{\prime,+}
of
K
containing
\varphi_{0}
).

Remark 2.4.2
.

If
F=\mathbb{Q}
, then
E(\varphi_{0})=K
. Indeed, then
\Phi^{\prime,+}=\Phi^{+}
is unique and
E_{\Phi^{+}}K=K
. If
F
is a quadratic extension, then there is another possibility for
\Phi^{\prime,+}
besides
\Phi^{+}
; then
E_{\Phi^{\prime,+}}K=E_{\Phi^{+}}K
is a quadratic extension of
K
and hence
E(\varphi_{0})
is a quadratic extension of
K
.

Here now is the second main result of this paper.

Theorem 2.4.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}})^{\wedge}
be the formal completion of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
along its special fiber, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{K_{v_{0}}}
. Then there exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}
,

(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{K_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}\simeq{J}_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\big].

The quotient on the right hand side is defined using an identification of the adjoint group
{J}_{\mathrm{U},{v_{0}},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
and through an action of
{J}_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}
. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{v_{0}}
, this isomorphism is compatible with the action of
\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
through Hecke correspondences on both sides.

We note that the extension of scalars from
K
to
E(\varphi_{0})
is a result of our proof, in the optimal result it should not appear.

Conjecture 2.4.4
.

The isomorphism in Theorem
2.4.3
is the base change by
\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}\Spf O_{K_{v_{0}}}
of an isomorphism

(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}})^{\wedge}\simeq{J}_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{{K_{v_{0}}}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\big].

We also extend this result to the case when
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}
is not necessarily parahoric by constructing an integral model
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}}
over
O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}
and proving a corresponding
p
-adic uniformization theorem in this context, cf. Theorem
10.2.1
.

As usual, the global results about integral models of Shimura curves follow from corresponding local results. We formulate as follows the main local result behind the previous global result, which we find quite striking.

Theorem 2.4.5
.

Let
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension. Let
K/F
be a quadratic extension, and let
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}
be an anisotropic
K/F
-hermitian group of size
2
over
F
. Fix an embedding
\varphi_{0}:K\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\mathrm{U}=\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{U}^{\prime})
and let
\mu
be defined as in the global case using a fixed local CM type
\Phi^{+}
with
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{+}
, with associated reflex field
K\subset\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
E(\varphi_{0})
be the extension of
K
in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, defined as in the global case. Also, let
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(a quasi-parahoric subgroup). Let
b
be a representative of the unique element
[b]\in B(\mathrm{U},\mu^{-1})
. Let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
be the associated integral local Shimura variety, cf.
[
22
]
. Then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
is a formal scheme over
O_{K}
and there is an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}\times_{\Spf O_{{K}}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})}\simeq\widehat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})}.

As in the global case, we conjecture that this isomorphism comes by base change from an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}\simeq\widehat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{K}.

Remark 2.4.6
.

We therefore obtain an explicit identification of the integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}}
for the case of the unitary group of an anisotropic hermitian space of dimension two over
F=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
:

(2.4.4)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}}=\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\times_{\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Spf O_{K}.

Previously, there were only two (indecomposable) cases, when an integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}}
was explicitly known: the Lubin-Tate case corresponding to
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
and
\mu=(1,0,\ldots,0)
and the unique basic element
[b]\in B(G,\mu^{-1})
and the maximal compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and the Drinfeld case corresponding to
G=D^{\times}_{1/n}
and
\mu=(1,0,\ldots,0)
and the unique basic element
[b]\in B(G,\mu^{-1})
and the maximal compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}=O^{\times}_{D_{1/n}}
. In these cases, the integral local Shimura varieties are of the form

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}}\times_{\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Spf\breve{}\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\Spf\breve{}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}]]\times\mathbb{Z}

and

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}}\times_{\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Spf\breve{}\mathbb{Z}_{p}=(\widehat{\Omega}^{n}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\times_{\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Spf\breve{}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{Z}

with explicit Weil descent data down to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Note that in these cases, the integral local Shimura varieties have infinitely many connected components as formal schemes over
{\breve{}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

Note that, by definition, integral local Shimura varieties are moduli spaces of shtukas, cf.
[
27
]
. However, in order to prove the explicit identifications of the above integral local Shimura varieties, we have to pass through moduli spaces of
p
-divisible groups. This is especially true of the Drinfeld case, in which case we have to appeal to
[
10
]
(see
[
3
]
,
[
25
]
for expositions of Drinfeld’s paper). It would be interesting to prove the explicit identifications directly from the definitions. Possibly the papers by S. Bartling
[
1
]
and A. Vanhaecke
[
28
]
are a step in this direction.

3.
CM-types and CM-triples

In this section we recall some notions that we will use throughout the paper.

3.1.
Special and banal local CM-types

We fix a prime number
p
and an algebraic closure
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}

of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
F
be a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with residue class field
\kappa_{F}
of cardinality
q
.
We set
d=[F:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]
,

f=[\kappa_{F}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]
and define
e
through
d=ef
.

We let
K/F
be an étale algebra of degree
2
. We
denote the non-trivial automorphism of
\Gal(K/F)
by
a\bar{a}
. In the case where
K/F
is a ramified extension of local fields
(ramified case) we choose a prime element
\Pi\in O_{K}

such that

\bar{\Pi}=-\Pi
. Then
\pi=-\Pi^{2}
is a prime element
of
F
. In the case where
K/F
is an unramified extension of local
fields (unramified case) or where
K\cong F\times F
(split case) we
choose a prime element
\pi\in F
and we set
\Pi=\pi
.

Let
m\in\{1,2\}
. Let
r
be a generalized local CM-type of rank
m
relative to

K/F
in the sense of
[
19
, Definition 2.1]
. Let
\Phi=\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}_{p}$-Alg}}(K,\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then
r
is a function

(3.1.1)

r:\Phi\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},\qquad\,\,\varphi r_{\varphi},

such that
r_{\varphi}+r_{\bar{\varphi}}=m
for all
\varphi\in\Phi
. Here
\bar{\varphi}(a)=\varphi(\bar{a})
(we recall that

a\bar{a}
is the non-trivial automorphism of
K
over
F
). In this paper, we refer to a generalized local CM-type of rank
m
relative to

K/F
simply as a local CM-type of rank
m
.
The reflex field
E_{r}
of
r
is the subfield of
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
fixed by

\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/E_{r}):=\{\tau\in\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mid r_{\tau\varphi}=r_{\varphi},\ \forall\varphi\}.

Let
O_{E_{r}}
be the ring of integers of
E_{r}
.

When we fix an embedding
\varphi_{0}:F\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we denote by
\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}
the two extensions of
\varphi_{0}
to
K
by abuse of notations.

Definition 3.1.1
.

A local CM-type
r
of rank
2
is called
special
relative to
\varphi_{0}:F\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
if
K/F
is a field extension and

r_{\varphi_{0}}=r_{\bar{\varphi}_{0}}=1,\text{ and }r_{\varphi}\in\{0,2\},\quad\text{ for all }\varphi\in\Phi\setminus\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}.

If
r_{\varphi}\in\{0,2\}
, for all
\varphi\in\Phi
, then we call
r
a
banal
CM-type. A local CM-type of rank
1
will always be regarded as a banal CM-type. There is a bijection between local CM-types of rank
1
and classical CM-types, via
\Phi^{+}(r)=\{\varphi\in\Phi\mid r_{\varphi}=1\}
.

3.2.
The Kottwitz and the Eisenstein conditions

Let
S
be an
O_{E_{r}}
-scheme, and
\mathcal{L}
be a locally free

\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module, equipped with an action

\iota:O_{K}\longrightarrow\End_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{L}

of
O_{K}
.

We say that
(\mathcal{L},\iota)
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
relative to
r
if the identity of polynomials with
coefficients in
\mathcal{O}_{S}
holds,

(3.2.1)

{\rm char}(T,\iota(a)|\mathcal{L})=i\big(\underset{\varphi\in\Phi}{\prod}(T-\varphi(a))^{r_{\varphi}}\big),\quad\text{ for all
$a\in O_{K}$ },

where
i:O_{E_{r}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}
is the structure homomorphism. We say
(\mathcal{L},\iota)
satisfies the rank condition
({\rm RC}_{r})
if the condition in
[
20
, (2.2.5)]
is satisfied and say
(\mathcal{L},\iota)
satisfies the Eisenstein condition
({\rm EC}_{r})
if
(\mathcal{L},\iota)
satisfies
({\rm RC}_{r})
and the conditions in
[
20
, (2.2.12)]
are fulfilled. Since we will not use the explicit form of the definition, we refer to
[
20
]
for a thorough discussion.

3.3.
Local CM-pairs and CM-triples

Let
r
be a local CM-type of rank
m\in\{1,2\}
, with reflex field
E_{r}
. Let
S
be an
O_{E_{r}}
-scheme such that
p
is locally nilpotent i.e. a scheme over
\Spf O_{E_{r}}
.
A
local CM-pair of type

r
with respect to
K/F
over
S
is a pair
(X,\iota)
where

X
is a
p
-divisible group over
S
of height
2md
and dimension
md
and

\iota
is an
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra homomorphism

\iota:O_{K}\longrightarrow\End X

such that the rank condition
({\rm RC}_{r})
is satisfied for the
induced action of
O_{K}
on
\Lie X
. In the split case

O_{K}=O_{F}\times O_{F}
we require moreover that in the induced
decomposition
X=X_{1}\times X_{2}
each factor is a
p
-divisible
group of height
md
. We say the pair
(X,\iota)
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
resp. the Eisenstein condition
({\rm EC}_{r})
if the
induced action of
O_{K}
on
\Lie X
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
resp. the Eisenstein condition
({\rm EC}_{r})
.

To each local CM-pair
(X,\iota)
we define the

conjugate dual

(X^{\vee},\iota^{\wedge})
. Here
X^{\vee}
is
the dual
p
-divisible group of
X
but we change the action dual to
\iota

by the conjugation of
K/F
, i.e.,
\iota^{\wedge}(a)=\iota^{\vee}(\bar{a})
. We
will denote the conjugate dual simply by
X^{\wedge}
. The conjugate dual of a CM-pair
(X,\iota)
of type
r
is again a local CM-pair of type
r
. If
(X,\iota)
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
, resp., the Eisenstein conditions
({\rm EC}_{r})
, then so does its conjugate dual. These assertions follow from
[
20
, Lemma 2.3.2]
.

The notion of a
local CM-triple of type

r
with respect to
K/F
over
S
was
introduced in
[
19
]
. This is a triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
,
where
(X,\iota)
is a local CM-pair of type
r
over
S
and

\lambda:X\longrightarrow X^{\wedge}
is an anti-symmetric isogeny (also
called a
polarization
) such that the corresponding Rosati involution
induces the non-trivial automorphism on
K/F
. In this paper, we will refer to a local CM-triple of type
r
with respect to
K/F
as a
local CM-triple of type
(K/F,r)
. We say the local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
resp. the Eisenstein condition
({\rm RC}_{r})
, if the local CM-pair
(X,\iota)
satisfies the Kottwitz condition
({\rm KC}_{r})
resp. the Eisenstein condition
({\rm RC}_{r})
.

Definition 3.3.1
.

We say that the local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
is
principally polarized
if the homomorphism
\lambda\colon XX^{\wedge}
is an isomorphism. We say that
(X,\iota,\lambda)
is
almost principally polarized
if
K/F
is unramified and the kernel of
\lambda_{X}
is contained in
X[\pi]
and has order
q^{2}
.

3.4.
Global and semi-local CM-triples

Let
K/F
now be a CM-field. Let
m\in\{1,2\}
. Let
r
be a
generalized CM-type of rank

m
, i.e., a function

(3.4.1)

r:\Phi\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},\qquad\,\,\varphi r_{\varphi},

such that
r_{\varphi}+r_{\bar{\varphi}}=m
for all
\varphi\in\Phi
. As in the local case, we will simply refer to a generalized CM-type of rank of
m
as a CM-type of rank
m
.

The corresponding reflex field
E_{r}
is the subfield of
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
fixed by

\Gal(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}/E_{r}):=\{\tau\in\Gal(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\mid r_{\tau\varphi}=r_{\varphi},\ \forall\varphi\}.

Let
O_{E_{r}}
be the ring of integers of
E_{r}
.

Definition 3.4.1
.

Let
w
be an archimedian place of
F
. We say that a CM-type of rank
2
is
special
with respect to
w
if for the extensions
\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}
of
w
we have
r_{\varphi_{0}}=r_{\bar{\varphi}_{0}}=1
and such that
r_{\varphi}\in\{0,2\}
for
\varphi\notin\{\varphi_{0},\bar{\varphi}_{0}\}
.

As in the local case, we can speak of
banal
CM-types. We always regard a CM-type of rank
1
as a banal CM-type. There is a bijection between CM-types of rank
1
and classical CM-types, via
\Phi^{+}(r)=\{\varphi\in\Phi\mid r_{\varphi}=1\}
.

Let
p
be a prime and let
v\mid p
be the
p
-adic places of
F
. We fix an embedding
\nu:\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Then we obtain a decomposition

\Phi=\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}$-Alg}}(K,\bar{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{v\mid p}\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}_{p}$-Alg}}(K_{v},\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{v\mid p}\Phi_{v}.

We denote by
r_{v}
the restriction of
r
to
\Phi_{v}
which is a local CM-type of rank
m
for
\Phi_{v}
. If
r
is a CM type of rank
1
which gives rise to a classical CM type
\Phi^{+}=\Phi^{+}(r)
, then
r_{v}
defines a classical local CM type

(3.4.2)

\Phi^{+}_{v}=\Phi^{+}_{v}(r_{v})\subset\Phi_{v}

such that
\Phi^{+}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{v\mid p}\Phi^{+}_{v}
.
Let
\nu
also denote the place of
E_{r}
over
p
induced by the embedding
\nu
. For each
v
, the reflex field of
r_{v}
is a subfield
E_{r}(v)
of
E_{r,\nu}
.

There is the obvious notion of a
semi-local CM-triple

(X,\iota,\lambda)
of type
r
with respect to

K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
Let
S
be an
O_{E_{r,\nu}}
-scheme such that
p
is locally nilpotent i.e.
a scheme over
\Spf O_{E_{r,\nu}}
.
Let
(X,\iota)
be a
p
-divisible
group over
S
with an action

\iota:O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow\End X.

The decomposition

O_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}O_{F_{v}}

induces the decomposition
X=\prod_{v\mid p}X_{v}
of the
p
-divisible group
X
. Let
\lambda
be a polarization of
X
which induces the conjugation
on
K/F
. Then the decomposition extends to

(3.4.3)

(X,\iota,\lambda)=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}(X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{v}).

We call
(X,\iota,\lambda)
a semi-local CM-triple of type
r
with respect to
K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
if each

(X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{v})
is a local
CM-triple of type
r_{v}
with respect to
K_{v}/F_{v}
. In the following, we will refer to a semi-local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
of type
r
with respect to
K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
simply as a
CM-triple of type
(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r)
over
S
. A CM-triple of type
(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r)
is said to satisfy the conditions
(\mathrm{KR}_{r})
and
(\mathrm{EC}_{r})
if each local CM-triple
(X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{v})
satisfies the conditions
(\mathrm{KR}_{r_{v}})
and
(\mathrm{EC}_{r_{v}})
.

4.
Formal moduli space of toric CM-triples

4.1.
Complex multiplication

Let
K/F
be a CM-field. Let
\Phi^{+}\subset\Phi=\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}$-Alg}}(K,\mathbb{C})

be a classical CM-type. We denote the reflex field of
\Phi^{+}
by
E
. We define an algebraic
torus
\mathrm{T}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, with
\mathbb{Q}
-valued points given by

\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})=\{a\in K^{\times}\;|\;a\bar{a}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\}.

We recall the reciprocity law. We define the homomorphism

\mu:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow(K\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C})^{\times}\cong\prod\nolimits_{\varphi\in\Phi}\mathbb{C}^{\times}.

The element
\mu(z)
, for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, has component
z
for

\varphi\in\Phi^{+}
and has component
1
for
\varphi\notin\Phi^{+}
. We find

\mu\bar{\mu}(z)=1\otimes z\in(K\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C})^{\times}
.
We obtain a homomorphism of algebraic tori

\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathbb{C}}.

This homomorphism is defined over
E
, and hence we obtain

\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{E}.

From this we define the
reflex norm map
by

(4.1.1)

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,E})\overset{\mu}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Res}_{E/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{T}_{E})\overset{\Nm_{E/\mathbb{Q}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}.

We consider over the algebraic closure
\bar{E}=\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
the set of
tuples
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)
, where
(A,\iota)
is an abelian
variety over
\bar{E}
of CM-type
\Phi^{+}
, endowed with a

\mathbb{Q}
-homogeneous polarization
\bar{\lambda}
such that its Rosati involution induces on
K

the conjugation over
F
and an isomorphism

\kappa:\hat{V}(A)\rightarrow V_{0}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}
of

K\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}
-modules. We call a second tuple

(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\bar{\lambda}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
equivalent to

(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)
if there is a quasi-isogeny

(4.1.2)

\alpha:(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda})\rightarrow(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\bar{\lambda}^{\prime})

such that the pull-back of
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
is
\bar{\lambda}
and the pull-back of
\bar{\kappa}^{\prime}
is
\bar{\kappa}
. In this case, we also say that
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)
is quasi-isogenous to
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\bar{\lambda^{\prime}},\kappa^{\prime})
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\Phi^{+}}
be the set of tuples
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)

up to equivalence.
Let
\sigma\in\Gal(\bar{E}/E)
. Taking the inverse image of

(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)
by

\hat{\sigma}:=\Spec\sigma:\Spec\bar{E}\rightarrow\Spec\bar{E}

gives a left action
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)\sigma(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)
of
\Gal(\bar{E}/E)
on
\mathcal{A}_{\Phi^{+}}
.

We formulate the main theorem of complex multiplication of Shimura and
Taniyama
[
9
, Theorem 4.19]
.

Theorem 4.1.1
.

The Galois group
\Gal(\bar{E}/E)
acts on
\mathcal{A}_{\Phi^{+}}
via its maximal
abelian quotient
\Gal({E}^{\rm ab}/E)
. Let

e\in(E\otimes\mathbb{A})^{\times}
and let

\mathrm{rec}(e)\in\Gal({E}^{\rm ab}/E)
be the automorphism given by the
reciprocity law of class field theory. The following tuples are equivalent:

\mathrm{rec}(e)(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\kappa)\equiv(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E}(e_{f})\kappa),

where
e_{f}
is the finite part of the idèle
e
.

Let
(E^{\times})^{\wedge}\subset(E\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f})^{\times}
be the closure
of
E^{\times}
. We deduce a homomorphism

(4.1.3)

\Gal(\bar{E}/E)\rightarrow(E\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f})^{\times}/(E^{\times})^{\wedge}\overset{\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}),

where the first arrow is deduced from class field reciprocity normalized such that the arithmetic Frobenius is sent to a uniformizer and the second
arrow exists because
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})^{\wedge}
. To see this last fact, we note that
the group of units in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})
is finite. Indeed, the units are elements of

K^{\times}
with all absolute values equal to
1
at all places including the
infinite ones. Therefore

\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q})^{\wedge}
by Chevalley’s theorem. Theorem
4.1.1
says
that the action of
\Gal(\bar{E}/E)
on
\mathcal{A}_{\Phi^{+}}
is via (
4.1.3
).
In the rest of this section, we discuss a local analog of this theorem.

4.2.
Formal moduli space for toric CM-triples

We fix a prime number
p
and an algebraic closure
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
F
be a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with residue class field
\kappa_{F}
.
Let
d=[F:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]=ef
where
e
is the ramification index and
f
is the inertia degree of
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We let
K/F
be an étale algebra of degree
2
. We denote the non-trivial element in
\Gal(K/F)
by
a\bar{a}
.

Let
\Phi=\Hom_{\text{$\mathbb{Q}_{p}$-Alg}}(K,\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
\Phi^{+}_{0}
be a classical local CM-type with respect to
K/F
, with corresponding local CM-type
r_{0}
of rank
1
. The reflex field of
r_{0}
will be denoted by
E_{0}=E(r_{0})
which is also the reflex field
E_{\Phi^{+}_{0}}
of the local CM-type
\Phi^{+}_{0}
. Let
\kappa_{E_{0}}
be the residue field of
E_{0}
.

We define
\mathrm{T}
to be the algebraic group torus over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{a\in K^{\times}:a\bar{a}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\}
. Then the maximal open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\{a\in O^{\times}_{K}:a\bar{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\}
.

Construction 4.2.1
.

Let
E
be a finite extension of
E_{0}
and
\Phi^{+}_{0}
be a classical local CM-type with respect to
K/F
. We define the
local reflex norm

(4.2.1)

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\longrightarrow\mathrm{T}

with respect to the data
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
similarly as in the global case. More precisely, we consider the homomorphism

\mu:E^{\times}\rightarrow(K\otimes E)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod\nolimits_{\Phi}E^{\times}

given by sending
a\in E^{\times}
to
a
for
\varphi\in\Phi^{+}_{0}
and to
1
for
\varphi\not\in\Phi^{+}_{0}
. This defines a morphism of tori

\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\longrightarrow\mathrm{T}_{E}.

The map
\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}
is the composite

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\overset{\mu}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Res}_{{E}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathrm{T}_{E}\xrightarrow{\Nm_{E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathrm{T}.

Let
\varpi
be a uniformizer of
E
. We define the element

(4.2.2)

w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}:=\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}(\varpi)\in\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

We refer to
w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}
as the
special element in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
for the uniformizer
\varpi
of
O_{E}
or simply as the special element in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
if the data
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
and
\varpi
are clear from the context.

If
E=E_{0}
and
r_{0}
is the local CM-type of rank
1
associated to a classical local CM-type
\Phi^{+}_{0}
, then we write
w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}
simply as
w_{r_{0}}
.

Remark 4.2.2
.

Note that the composite of

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}:E^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K^{\times}

with the norm map
\Nm_{K/F}
is given by
\Nm_{E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. In particular, we see that
\Nm_{K/F}(w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E})
agrees with
p^{f_{E}}
up to unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Here
f_{E}
denotes the inertia index of
E
.

Let
E^{\prime}/E
be a finite extension with ring of integers
O_{E^{\prime}}
and inertia degree
f_{E^{\prime}}
. Let
w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E^{\prime}}
be the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E^{\prime})
for a uniformizer
\varpi^{\prime}
of
E^{\prime}
. Then
\Nm_{K/F}(w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E^{\prime}})=p^{f_{E^{\prime}}}
up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and hence
\Nm_{K/F}(w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E^{\prime}})
agrees with
\Nm_{K/F}(w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E})^{f_{E^{\prime}}/f_{E}}
up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. It follows that
w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E^{\prime}}
differs from
(w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E})^{f_{E^{\prime}}/f_{E}}
by an element in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Definition 4.2.3
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the functor
\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}
. Fix a local CM-triple
(\mathbb{X}_{0},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
over
\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}}
, satisfying the condition
(\mathrm{KC}_{r_{0}})
. For a scheme
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
, a point of
\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)(S)
is given by the following data:

(1)

A local CM-triple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
over
S
satisfying the condition
(\mathrm{KC}_{r_{0}})
;

(2)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:\bar{X}_{0}:=X_{0}\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{0}\times_{\Spec k}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
S
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\rho_{0}^{*}({\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=up^{i}\cdot{\lambda}_{0}
on
\bar{X}_{0}
.

Two tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
and
(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\rho^{\prime}_{0})
define the same point in
\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)
if and only if there is an isomorphism
\alpha_{0}:(X_{0},\iota_{0})\rightarrow(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0})
such that
\rho^{\prime}_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}=\rho_{0}
. Then
\alpha^{\ast}_{0}(\lambda_{0}^{\prime})=u\lambda_{0}
for some
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

The formal scheme representing this functor is also denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)
. We define the formal scheme
\mathcal{M}_{0}
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
by

(4.2.3)

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}:=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0}(i).

Then
\mathcal{M}_{0}
parametrizes tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
such that
\rho_{0}^{*}({\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=\nu\lambda_{0}
with
\nu\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
. Let

(4.2.4)

J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{\alpha_{0}\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbb{X}_{0})\mid\alpha_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=\mu_{0}(\alpha_{0})\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\,\,\mu_{0}(\alpha_{0})\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\}.

Then
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts naturally on
\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0})
.

We have the polarized contraction functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r_{0}}^{\rm pol}
in
[
20
, Theorem 4.5.11]
, trivially modified to the case when
r_{0}
is of rank
1
. It associates to a local CM triple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
over a scheme
S\in\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}
a triple
(C,\iota,\phi)
where
C
is a
p
-adic étale sheaf in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules over
S
with
\rank_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C=2d
(where we recall
d=[F:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]
) with an
O_{K}
-action
\iota:O_{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(C)
, and where
\phi:C\times C\rightarrow O_{F}
is an
O_{F}
-bilinear pairing on
C
such that
\phi(\iota(a)c_{1},c_{2})=\phi(c_{1},\iota(\bar{a})c_{2})
for
c_{1},c_{2}\in C
and
a\in O_{K}
. Let
(\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
be the image of
(\mathbb{X}_{0},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})
under this functor. Here
\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
is regarded as the constant
sheaf on
S
associated to an
O_{K}
-module
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\simeq O_{K}
described similarly as in
[
20
, Remark 4.5.12]
and
[
20
, (7.4.14)]
(trivially modified to the case when
r_{0}
is a local CM-type of rank
1
).

Definition 4.2.4
.

We consider the following functor
\mathcal{G}_{0}(i)
on the category of schemes
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
. A point of
\mathcal{G}_{0}(i)(S)
is given
by the following data:

(1)

A locally
constant
p
-adic étale sheaf
C
on
S
which is

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-flat with
\rank_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C=2d
and with an action

\iota:O_{K}\longrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C;

(2)

A perfect alternating
O_{F}
-bilinear pairing

\phi:C\times C\longrightarrow O_{F},

such that
\phi(\iota(a)c_{1},c_{2})=\phi(c_{1},\iota(\bar{a})c_{2})
for

c_{1},c_{2}\in C
and
a\in O_{K}
;

(3)

A quasi-isogeny of
O_{K}
-module sheaves on
S

\rho:(C,\iota)\longrightarrow(\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\iota_{0}).

Another set of data
(C^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
defines the same point
iff there is an isomorphism
\alpha:CC^{\prime}
such that

\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha=\rho
. Then
\alpha
respects
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
up
to a factor in
O_{F}^{\times}
.

The existence of the quasi-isogeny implies that
C
is locally
constant for the Zariski topology. Therefore locally on
S
the sheaf

C
is the constant sheaf associated to an
O_{K}
-submodule

C\subset C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\rho
is
given by the last inclusion.

The polarized contraction functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r_{0}}^{\rm pol}
in
[
20
, Theorem 4.5.11]
defines a isomorphism of functors

(4.2.5)

\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)\mathcal{G}_{0}(i).

To describe the functor
\mathcal{G}_{0}(i)
, we may restrict to the case
where the sheaf
C
is given by an
O_{K}
-submodule of

C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
Then
C
defines a point of
\mathcal{G}_{0}(i)(S)
iff

(1/p^{i})\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
is a perfect alternating pairing on
C
.
We define an algebraic group over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
whose
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-rational points are given by

\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{O_{K}}(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})\;|\;\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=u\cdot\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2})\;\text{for some}\;u\in O^{\times}_{F}\}.

Since all such
C
are isomorphic to
O_{K}
, cf.
[
20
, 7.4.14]
, there is an isomorphism

(4.2.6)

g:(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})\longrightarrow(C,\frac{1}{p^{i}}\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}).

This means that
gC_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}=C
and

(4.2.7)

\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=p^{i}\cdot\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2}),\;\quad c_{1},c_{2}\in C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

We define

\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(i)=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{K}(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;|\;\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=p^{i}u\cdot\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2}),\;\;u\in O^{\times}_{F}\}.

This construction gives us a functor isomorphism

\mathcal{G}_{0}(i)\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(i)/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where the right hand side is considered as the restriction of the constant
sheaf to
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}
. Hence we obtain an isomorphism

(4.2.8)

\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(i)/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Let
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\mathrm{GL}_{K}(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the union of
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(i)
. Using the
functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r_{0}}^{\rm pol}
, we may write

(4.2.9)

\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{\alpha\in\Aut_{K}\mathbb{X}_{0}\;|\;\alpha^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=\mu(\alpha)\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\;\text{for some}\;\mu(\alpha)\in p^{\mathbb{Z}}O^{\times}_{F}\}.

Therefore
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts via
\rho
on the formal scheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
.
The isomorphism of functors on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}
obtained from (
4.2.5
) and (
4.2.8
) is then a
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant isomorphism,

(4.2.10)

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Now we define

(4.2.11)

\mathrm{T}(i)=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{K}(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;|\;\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=p^{i}f\cdot\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2}),\;\text{for some}\;f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\}.

Using an identification
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\simeq O_{K}
, we have
