\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{T}(i)

and, similarly,

\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{O_{K}}(C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})\;|\;\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=f\cdot\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2})\;\text{for some}\;f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\}.

From this description, it is clear that
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Using (
4.2.6
) and (
4.2.7
), it is easy to see that
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(i)/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is in bijection with
\mathrm{T}(i)/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Thus we have by (
4.2.8
)

(4.2.12)

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(i)\mathrm{T}(i)/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

and hence an isomorphism

(4.2.13)

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

which is equivariant for the action of
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

Next we discuss the Weil descent datum on
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
from
O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
down to
O_{E_{0}}
. Let
\tau_{E_{0}}\in\Gal(\breve{E}_{0}/E_{0})
be the Frobenius. It is enough to restrict the functor
\mathcal{M}_{0}

to affine schemes
S=\Spec R
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
. We write

\varepsilon:O_{\breve{E}_{0}}\longrightarrow R
for the given algebra structure.
We write
R_{[\tau_{E_{0}}]}
for the ring
R
with the new algebra structure

\varepsilon\circ\tau_{E_{0}}
.
By base change to
\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}}
, we obtain

\bar{\varepsilon}:\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}}\longrightarrow\bar{R}:=R\otimes_{O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}}.

Let
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
be a point of
\mathcal{M}_{0}(\bar{R})
where
\rho_{0}
is a quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:X_{0,\bar{R}}\longrightarrow\bar{\varepsilon}_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}=\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}}.

Since the notion of a CM-triple depends only on the induced
O_{E}
-algebra structure on
R
, we may regard
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})

as a CM-triple on
R_{[\tau_{E_{0}}]}
. We set

(4.2.14)

\tilde{\rho}_{0}:X_{0}\xrightarrow{\rho_{0}}\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X}_{0},\tau_{E_{0}}}}\tau_{E_{0}\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}}.

The assignment

(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\tilde{\rho}_{0})

defines a morphism

(4.2.15)

\omega_{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}}:\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)(\bar{R})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0}(i+f_{E_{0}})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E_{0}}]}).

Here we note that the inverse image
of the polarization
(\tau_{E_{0}})_{\ast}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
on

(\tau_{E_{0}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}
by the Frobenius isogeny

F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{0}}}:\mathbb{X}_{0}\longrightarrow(\tau_{E_{0}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}

is given by
p^{f_{E_{0}}}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
. Then (
4.2.15
) defines the Weil descent datum

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}:\mathcal{M}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(\tau_{E_{0}})}.

Proposition 4.2.5
.

There is an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

which is equivariant for the action of
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

The above Weil descent datum on
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
corresponds on the right-hand side to the Weil descent datum given by multiplication by the special element
w_{r_{0}}
in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E_{0})
for a uniformizer
\varpi_{0}
.

Proof.

The isomorphism

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

is already proved in (
4.2.13
). Recall from the beginning of §
3.1
the meaning of
\Pi
and
\pi
. We define the element
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}\in\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by the following recipe:

(1)

If
K/F
is ramified, we define
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
as the multiplication

\Pi^{ef_{E_{0}}}:C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\longrightarrow C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p};

(3)

If
K/F
is unramified, we define
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
as the multiplication

\pi^{ef_{E_{0}}/2}:C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\longrightarrow C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p},

where we note that
ef_{E_{0}}
is even by the same computation as in
[
20
, Lemma 6.4.4]
.

(4)

If
K=F\times F
is split, we have the decomposition

C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=C_{\mathbb{X}_{0},1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\oplus C_{\mathbb{X}_{0},2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

We consider the numbers

a_{1}
and
a_{2}
defined by
\dim\mathbb{X}_{0,1}=a_{1}
and
\dim\mathbb{X}_{0,2}=a_{2}
and set

a_{1,E_{0}}=a_{1}\frac{f_{E_{0}}}{f},\quad\text{ resp. }\quad a_{2,E_{0}}=a_{2}\frac{f_{E_{0}}}{f}.

Then we have
a_{1,E_{0}}+a_{2,E_{0}}=ef_{E_{0}}
. We define
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
to be the multiplication by
\pi^{a_{1,E_{0}}}
on
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0},1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and the multiplication by
\pi^{a_{2,E_{0}}}
on
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0},2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Then by the same calculation on Dieudonné modules as in
[
20
, Propositions 6.3.2, 6.4.3, 6.5.1]
adapted to the case of rank
1
local CM-types, the natural descent datum on the left-hand side of

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

corresponds on the right-hand side to the Weil descent datum given by multiplication by
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
. Indeed, for example, suppose
K/F
is ramified. Let
\tau
be the Frobenius automorphism of
\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, then there is an identification

C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}=C_{\tau_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}}
. We apply the functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r_{0}}^{\rm pol}
to the Frobenius isogeny

F_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}:\mathbb{X}_{0}\longrightarrow\tau_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}
. Consider the Dieudonné module
P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
, we have

C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}=\{c\in P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\;|\;Vc=\Pi^{e}c\}
.
The map

P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\longrightarrow W(\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}})\otimes_{\tau,W(\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}})}P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\quad c1\otimes c

defines the identification
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}=C_{\tau_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}}
. The Frobenius
F_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
induces on the Dieudonné modules

P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\overset{V^{\sharp}}{\longrightarrow}W(\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}})\otimes_{\tau,W(\bar{\kappa}_{E_{0}})}P_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\quad x1\otimes Vx.

For
c\in C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
we obtain
V^{\sharp}c=1\otimes Vc=1\otimes\Pi^{e}c
. Therefore
F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{0}}}:\mathbb{X}_{0}\longrightarrow(\tau_{E_{0}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}
corresponds to multiplication by
\Pi^{ef_{E_{0}}}
on
C_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
. The other cases follow similarly.

Since the element
w_{r_{0}}
has norm
\Nm_{K/F}(w_{r_{0}})=p^{f_{E_{0}}}u
for some unit
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
has norm
\Nm_{K/F}(w^{\prime}_{r_{0}})=\pi^{ef_{E_{0}}}
when we view them as elements in
K^{\times}
, they differ by a unit in
O^{\times}_{K}
.
Therefore, in the bijection

\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

the class of
w_{r_{0}}
is sent to the class of
w^{\prime}_{r_{0}}
and hence the descent datum is given as in the statement of the proposition.
∎

Remark 4.2.6
.

Let
E
be a finite extension of
E_{0}
and
O_{E}
be its ring of integers. Let
f_{E}
be the inertia degree of
E
and
\varpi
be a uniformizer of
O_{E}
, then it is clear from the proof of the above proposition and Remark
4.2.2
that we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and the Weil descent datum on the left hand side relative to
O_{\breve{E}}/O_{E}
is given on the right-hand side by multiplication by the special element
w_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}\in\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for the uniformizer
\varpi
.

Part II
The RSZ unitary Shimura curves

5.
Formal moduli space of RSZ CM-triples

Let
F
be a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with residue class field
\kappa_{F}
.
We set
d=[F:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]
,

f=[\kappa_{F}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]
and define
e
through
d=ef
.
We let
K/F
be an étale algebra of degree
2
.

Let
r
be a local CM-type of rank
2
relative to
K/F
whose reflex field is denoted by
E_{r}
. Let
\Phi^{+}_{0}
be a classical local CM-type relative to
K/F
whose reflex field is denoted by
E_{0}
and let
r_{0}
be the local CM-type of rank
1
associated to
\Phi^{+}_{0}
. Let
E=E_{0}E_{r}
be the composite field of
E_{0}
and
E_{r}
. We denote by
O_{E}
the valuation ring of
E
and by
f_{E}
the inertia degree of
E
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\kappa=\kappa_{E}
be residue class field of
E
. We fix a uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
of
E
.

We define
\mathrm{T}
to be the torus over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{a\in K^{\times}\colon a\bar{a}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\}
.
Let
V
be a free
K
-module of rank
2
equipped with an alternating
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear form

(5.0.1)

\varsigma\colon V\times V\mathbb{Q}_{p}

such that

(5.0.2)

\varsigma(ax,y)=\varsigma(x,\bar{a}y),\quad x,y\in V,\,a\in K.

There is a unique anti-hermitian form
\varkappa
on
V
such that

(5.0.3)

\Trace_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}a\varkappa(x,y)=\varsigma(ax,y),\quad x,y\in V,\,a\in K.

Conversely, the anti-hermitian form
\varkappa
determines the alternating bilinear form
\varsigma
with (
5.0.2
). We say that
\varkappa
arises from
\varsigma
by contraction.
Let
\Lambda
be an almost selfdual
O_{K}
-lattice in
V
(see the Notation section for this term). Let
\mathrm{U}(V)
be the unitary group over
F
for the anti-hermitian space
(V,\varkappa)
and set
\mathrm{U}=\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{U}(V))
. We define the algebraic group
\widetilde{G}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
\widetilde{G}=\mathrm{U}\times\mathrm{T}
. Let
G=\mathrm{GU}(V,\varsigma)
be the unitary similitude group of
V
with similitude factor in
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Then
\widetilde{G}
is also isomorphic to the fiber product group
G\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathrm{T}
, similarly as in the global case, cf. (
2.1.9
).

Let
(X,\iota,\lambda)
be a local CM triple of type
r
, cf. §
3.3
. We say that
(X,\iota,\lambda)
is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized if
(X,\iota,\lambda)
is principally polarized unless
K/F
is unramified and
{\mathrm{inv}}(V)=-1
, in which case it is almost principally polarized, cf. Definition
3.3.1
.

5.1.
Formal moduli space of RSZ CM-triples: the special case

In this subsection, we will study a formal moduli space of CM-triples associated to the group
\widetilde{G}
and describe it in terms of the Drinfeld upper-half plane with its descent datum. We assume that
K/F
is a field extension.

We fix an embedding
\varphi_{0}:K\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We suppose in this subsection that the local CM-type
r
is special relative to
{\varphi_{0}}_{|F}
, cf. Definition
3.1.1
. We also let
r_{0}
be a local CM-type of rank
1
whose associated local CM-type is denoted by
\Phi^{+}_{0}
. We assume that
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{+}_{0}
.

In this subsection, we will assume that
V
is anisotropic, i.e.,
{\mathrm{inv}}(V)=-1
. Therefore
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}:=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a maximal quasi-parahoric. Let
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\{a\in O^{\times}_{K}:a\bar{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\}
be the maximal open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which we will also denote by
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
. Let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}
, it is a maximal open compact subgroup of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Note that we have

(5.1.1)

\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}\simeq\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}\simeq\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}.

We fix a local CM-triple
(\mathbb{X}_{0},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
over
\bar{\kappa}
satisfying the condition
({\rm KC}_{r_{0}})
and a local CM triple
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
of type
(K/F,r)
over
\bar{\kappa}
which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized. We also assume that
{\mathrm{inv}}^{r}(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=-1={\mathrm{inv}}(V)
.

Definition 5.1.1
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the functor
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}}}
. For a scheme
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
, a point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(S)
consists of the following data:

(1)

A local CM-triple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
satisfying the condition
({\rm KC}_{r_{0}})
;

(2)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:\bar{X}_{0}:=X_{0}\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{0}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that locally on
\bar{S}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=up^{i}\lambda_{0}
;

(3)

A local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X})
of type

(K/F,r)
over
S

which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized;

(4)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=up^{i}{\lambda_{\bar{X}}}
, locally on
\bar{S}
, with the same
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
as in datum
(2)
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
. Two tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
and
(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\rho^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda_{X^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime})
define the same point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
iff there exist isomorphisms of
O_{K}
-modules
\alpha_{0}:(X_{0},\iota_{0})(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0})
and
\alpha:(X,\iota)(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
such that
\rho^{\prime}_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,\bar{S}}=\rho_{0}
and
\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha_{\bar{S}}=\rho
.

Remark 5.1.2
.

We could replace the condition in
(2)
by
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=p^{i}\lambda_{0}
and the condition in
(4)
by
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=p^{i}\lambda_{X}
without changing the formal moduli space
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
. We could also replace the condition in
(2)
by the condition that
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})
differs from
p^{i}{\lambda}_{0}
on
\bar{X}_{0}
by a unit
u
in
O_{F}
and the condition in
(4)
by the condition that
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
differs from
p^{i}{\lambda}_{\bar{X}}
on
\bar{X}
by the same unit. This follows from the same reasoning as in
[
20
, Remark 7.2.3]
.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
. We define the formal scheme

(5.1.2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i).

Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
parametrizes tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
such that
\rho_{0}
respects the polarization
\lambda_{0}
on
\bar{X}_{0}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
on
\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{S}}
up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
and
\rho
respects the polarization
\lambda_{\bar{X}}
on
\bar{X}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}_{\bar{S}}
up to the same factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
.

We define

(5.1.3)

\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{0}\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbb{X}_{0})\mid\alpha_{0}^{\ast}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}=\mu_{0}(\alpha_{0})\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\,\,\mu_{0}(\alpha_{0})\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\};

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbb{X})\mid\alpha^{\ast}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}=\mu(\alpha)\lambda_{\mathbb{X}},\,\,\mu(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\};

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
affords an action of the group
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via the framing data
\rho
and
\rho_{0}
in
(4)
and
(2)
by

\widetilde{\alpha}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha):(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho).

Note that by
[
20
, Lemma 5.2.2, Lemma 5.3.2]
,
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the group of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-points of a unitary similitude group
J
with rational similitude factor of a anti-hermitian space whose invariant is
+1
, cf.
[
20
, (7.2.6)]
. Also,
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, see (
4.2.11
). Therefore
\widetilde{J}=J\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathrm{T}
is an inner form of
\widetilde{G}
such that
\widetilde{J}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is isomorphic to
\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}({\mathrm{PGL}}_{2})
.

Next, we consider the Weil descent datum on
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
. It is enough to restrict the functor
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
to affine schemes
S=\Spec R
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
. We write

\varepsilon:O_{\breve{E}}\longrightarrow R
for the given algebra structure. Let
\tau_{E}
be the Frobenius automorphism of
\bar{\kappa}
over
\kappa
. We write
R_{[\tau_{E}]}
for the ring
R
with the new algebra structure

\varepsilon\circ\tau_{E}
.
By base change to
\bar{\kappa}
, we obtain
\bar{\varepsilon}:\bar{\kappa}\longrightarrow\bar{R}:=R\otimes_{O_{\breve{E}}}\bar{\kappa}
.

Let
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
be a point of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R})
where
\rho_{0}
and
\rho
are quasi-isogenies

\rho_{0}:X_{0,\bar{R}}\longrightarrow\bar{\varepsilon}_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}=\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}},\quad\text{ resp.}\quad\rho:X_{\bar{R}}\longrightarrow\bar{\varepsilon}_{\ast}\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}.

We set

\tilde{\rho}_{0}:X_{0,\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{0}}\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X}_{0},\tau_{E}}}\tau_{E\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{R}},\quad\text{ resp.}\quad\tilde{\rho}:X_{\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}}\tau_{E\ast}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}.

The assignment

(5.1.4)

(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\tilde{\rho}_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\tilde{\rho})

defines a map

(5.1.5)

\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(\bar{R})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]}).

Here we note that the inverse image
of the polarization
(\tau_{E})_{\ast}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
on

(\tau_{E})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}
by the Frobenius isogeny

F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}:\mathbb{X}\longrightarrow(\tau_{E})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}

is
p^{f_{E}}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
and similarly for
\mathbb{X}_{0}
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
.

From (
5.1.5
) we obtain the Weil descent datum

(5.1.6)

\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\longrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(\tau_{E})}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}},

where the upper index
(\tau_{E})
denotes the base change via

\Spec\tau_{E}:\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\longrightarrow\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
.

We recall from
[
20
]
the definition of the formal moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}_{r}
of local CM-triples associated to the group
G
, cf.
[
20
, Definition 2.6.1]
. We denote by
O_{E_{r}}
the valuation ring of
E_{r}
and by
f_{E_{r}}
the inertia degree of
E_{r}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\kappa_{r}
be residue class field of
E_{r}
.

Definition 5.1.3
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the functor
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{r}}}
. For a scheme
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
, a point of
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)(S)
consists of the following data:

(1)

A local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X})
of type

(K/F,r)
over
S
which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized;

(2)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}_{r}}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
\bar{S}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
with
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=up^{i}{\lambda}_{\bar{X}}
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
. Two tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
and
(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda_{X^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime})
define the same point of
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
iff there exists an isomorphism of
O_{K}
-modules
\alpha:(X,\iota)(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
such that
\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha_{\bar{S}}=\rho
. Note that the requirement on
\rho
in
(2)
above implies that

2\mathrm{height}(\rho)=\mathrm{height}(p^{i}\mid X)=4di.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
.
We define the formal scheme
\mathcal{M}_{r}
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
by

(5.1.7)

\mathcal{M}_{r}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{r}(i).

Then
\mathcal{M}_{r}
parametrizes tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
such that
\rho
respects the polarization
\lambda_{\bar{X}}
on
\bar{X}
and

{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}
up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
. We define a map

(5.1.8)

\mathfrak{v}_{r}:{\mathcal{M}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}

sending the tuple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
to
(2d)^{-1}\mathrm{height}(\rho)
and hence
\mathfrak{v}_{r}({\mathcal{M}}_{r}(i))=i
. The group
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts naturally on
\mathcal{M}_{r}
via the framing
\rho
by
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho)
.

Next, we consider the Weil descent datum on
\mathcal{M}_{r}
. It is enough to restrict the functor
\mathcal{M}_{r}
to an affine scheme
S=\Spec R
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
. We write

\varepsilon:O_{\breve{E}_{r}}\longrightarrow R
for the given algebra structure. Let
\tau_{E_{r}}
be the Frobenius automorphism of
\bar{\kappa}_{r}
over
\kappa_{r}
. We write
R_{[\tau_{E_{r}}]}
for the ring
R
with the new algebra structure

\varepsilon\circ\tau_{E_{r}}
.
By base change to
\bar{\kappa}_{r}
, we obtain

\bar{\varepsilon}:\bar{\kappa}_{r}\longrightarrow\bar{R}:=R\otimes_{O_{\breve{E}_{r}}}\bar{\kappa_{r}}.

Let
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
be a point of
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\bar{R})
and we set

\tilde{\rho}:X_{\bar{R}}\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{r}}}}\tau_{E_{r}\ast}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}.

The assignment

(5.1.9)

(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\tilde{\rho})

defines a map

(5.1.10)

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}:\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)(\bar{R})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}(i+f_{E_{r}})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E_{r}}]}).

Here we note that the inverse image
of the polarization
(\tau_{E_{r}})_{\ast}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
on

(\tau_{E_{r}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}
by the Frobenius isogeny

F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{r}}}:\mathbb{X}\longrightarrow(\tau_{E_{r}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}

is
p^{f_{E_{r}}}\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
. Then (
5.1.10
) defines the Weil descent datum

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}:\mathcal{M}_{r}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}^{(\tau_{E_{r}})}.

We fix an isomorphism
J_{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F)
. Then
{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on
\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
via this isomorphism. We extend this to an action of
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}})\times\mathbb{Z}
by the translation by
\val(\mu(\alpha))
for
\alpha\in J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on the second factor.

Proposition 5.1.4
.

There exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}

\mathcal{M}_{r}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}})\times\mathbb{Z}

which is equivariant for the action of
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and such that the Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}
on the left hand side induces on the right hand side the Weil descent datum

(\xi,i)(\omega_{\Omega,E_{r}}(\xi),i+f_{E_{r}}).

Here
\omega_{\Omega,E_{r}}
denotes the action of
\tau_{E_{r}}
on the formal scheme
\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
through the second factor.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
20
, Corollary 6.1.3]
in the case when
K/F
is ramified and in
[
20
, Corollary 6.2.5]
when
K/F
is unramified.
∎

Recall the space
\mathcal{M}_{0}(i)
in Definition
4.2.3
and the space
\mathcal{M}_{0}
in (
4.2.3
). We have a morphism of formal moduli spaces over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}

(5.1.11)

\vartheta:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}},

given by sending
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
to
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)\times(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
. Here

\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}=\mathcal{M}_{r}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}},\quad{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{0}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}},

with their induced Weil descent data
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}
down to
O_{E}
. Also, there is an action
of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on the right-hand side via the map
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Lemma 5.1.5
.

The morphism
\vartheta
induces a closed immersion of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
,

\vartheta:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}.

In fact

\vartheta:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i),\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}.

Furthermore,
\vartheta
is equivariant for the action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and is compatible with the natural Weil descent data down to
O_{E}
on both sides.

Proof.

By Remark
5.1.2
, the space
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
can be identified with the fiber product

\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i).

Hence
\vartheta
is a closed embedding of formal schemes. The equivariance claim is obvious.

Let
\Spec R
be a scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
. We need to verify the commutativity of the following diagram,

(5.1.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}(i+f_{E})\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{{\mathcal{M}}_{r},E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta}

But this is clear by construction.
∎

We consider the local reflex norm (
4.2.1
) with respect to the datum
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
:

(5.1.13)

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\longrightarrow\mathrm{T}.

Let

(5.1.14)

\widetilde{w}={\mathfrak{r}}_{\Phi^{+}_{0},E}(\varpi_{E})\in\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

be the special element in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
as in Construction
4.2.1
.

Proposition 5.1.6
.

There is an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}

compatible with natural actions of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides. Here
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on
(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}
via the composition

\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F)

on the first factor and via
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by multiplication on the second factor (which we identify with
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
, cf. (
5.1.1
)).

The Weil descent datum
\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}

on the left-hand side corresponds on the
right-hand side to the Weil descent datum given by

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,E}(\xi),\widetilde{w}g),\quad g\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

where
\widetilde{w}
is the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
for our fixed uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
, viewed as an element in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proof.

We first construct an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}.

We consider the composite map
\mathcal{M}_{r}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}})\times\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
, where the last map is the projection to the second factor. By the construction of Proposition
5.1.4
, this map maps
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
to
i
. Therefore we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\times\mathbb{Z}\big)\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\big)\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\big)\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}.

The equivariance claim is obvious. Let
\Spec R
be a scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
. From Lemma
5.1.5
and Proposition
5.1.4
, we obtain the commutativity of the following diagram

(5.1.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\times\{i\}\big)\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\times\{i+f_{E}\}\big)\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\Omega,E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the right vertical map is given under the natural isomorphism

\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\times\{j\}\big)\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(j)\simeq\big(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\big)\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(j)

simply by
\omega_{\Omega,{E}}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}
. Finally, by Proposition
4.2.5
, the descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}
corresponds to multiplication by the special element
\widetilde{w}
under the isomorphism
\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}\simeq\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

5.2.
Formal moduli space of RSZ CM-triples: the banal case

Let
r
be a local CM-type of rank
2
. We now assume that
r
is banal, i.e.,
r_{\varphi}\in\{0,2\}
for all
\varphi\in\Phi
. Let
r_{0}
still be a local CM-type of rank
1
with associated local CM-type
\Phi^{+}_{0}
.

Let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
still be the maximal open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
but now let
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
be an arbitrary open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
, it is an open compact subgroup of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. The open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
defines an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{G}
of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as the image of
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
under the map
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We fix a local CM-triple
(\mathbb{X}_{0},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
over
\bar{\kappa}
satisfying the condition
({\rm KC}_{r_{0}})
and a local CM-triple
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
of type
(K/F,r)
over
\bar{\kappa}
which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized. We make the assumption
{\mathrm{inv}}^{r}(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})={\mathrm{inv}}(V)
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
a functor
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}}}
. For a scheme
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
, a point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(S)
consists of the following data:

(1)

A local CM-triple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
of type
(K/F,r_{0})
satisfying the condition
({\rm KC}_{r_{0}})
;

(2)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:\bar{X}_{0}:=X_{0}\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{0}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
\bar{S}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
with
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=up^{i}{\lambda}_{0}
;

(3)

A local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X})
of type
(K/F,r)
over
S

which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized;

(4)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=up^{i}{\lambda_{\bar{X}}}
, locally on
\bar{S}
, with the same
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
as in datum
(2)
;

(5)

A class

\bar{\eta}
of isomorphisms of étale sheaves

\eta:(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X),\mathfrak{e})(\Lambda,\varsigma)\;\mathrm{mod}\;\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}

which respect the bilinear forms on both sides.
Here
(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X)
is the
p
-adic étale sheaf of
[
20
, Definition 7.4.1]
, and the bilinear form
\mathfrak{e}:\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X)\times\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is defined as in
[
20
, Proposition 7.4.7]
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})
. We will call datum
(5)
a
strict CL-level structure
on
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
. Two tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
and
(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\rho^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda_{X^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime})
define the same point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
if there exist isomorphisms of
O_{K}
-modules
\alpha_{0}:(X_{0},\iota_{0})(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0})
and
\alpha:(X,\iota)(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
such that
\rho^{\prime}_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,\bar{S}}=\rho_{0}
and
\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha_{\bar{S}}=\rho
, and which pulls back
\bar{\eta^{\prime}}
to
\bar{\eta}
.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
. We note that Remark
5.1.2
applies to this formal moduli space.
We define the formal scheme

(5.2.1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i).

Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
parametrizes tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})
such that
\rho_{0}
respects the polarization
\lambda
on
\bar{X}_{0}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
on
\mathbb{X}_{0,\bar{S}}
up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
and
\rho
respects the polarization
\lambda_{\bar{X}}
on
\bar{X}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}_{\bar{S}}
up to the same factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
.

The framing objects define as in (
5.1.3
) the groups
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Note, as in the special case, that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
affords an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. However, in the present banal case
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to the unitary similitude group
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, cf.
[
20
, (7.2.8)]
. Also, as before,
J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, see (
4.2.11
). Therefore, in the banal case, we have an isomorphism
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
, the group
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on the tower of formal schemes
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
in the usual way.

Let
S=\Spec R
be an affine scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
. The same formula as in (
5.1.9
) defines the map

(5.2.2)

\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(\bar{R})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]})

and hence defines the Weil descent datum

(5.2.3)

\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}\longrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(\tau_{E})}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}}

on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
.

We recall the definition of the formal moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
of local CM triples of type
(K/F,r)
when
r
is banal, cf.
[
20
, Definition 7.4.3]
.

Definition 5.2.2
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
a functor
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{r}}}
. For a scheme
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
, a point of
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i)(S)
consists of the following data:

(1)

A local CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X})
of type

(K/F,r)
over
S
which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized;

(2)

An
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
\bar{S}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
with
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=up^{i}{\lambda}_{\bar{X}}
;

(3)

A class

\bar{\eta}_{X}
of isomorphisms of étale sheaves

{\eta}_{X}:(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0},X),\mathfrak{e}_{X})(\Lambda,\varsigma)\;\mathrm{mod}\;\mathbf{K}_{G}

which respect the bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Here
\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}
is the lift of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
over
O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
as in
[
20
, (7.4.14)]
(where it is denoted by
X_{0}
) and
(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0},X)
is the
p
-adic étale sheaf of
[
20
, Definition 7.4.1]
and we define the alternating form
\mathfrak{e}_{X}
as in
[
20
, Proposition 7.4.7]
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
. The datum
(3)
is called a
(relaxed) CL-level structure
on
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
. Here we sometimes use the qualifier “relaxed” to distinguish from a strict CL-level structure. Two tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
and
(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda_{X^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}})
define the same point of
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i)
iff there exists an isomorphism of
O_{K}
-modules
\alpha:(X,\iota)(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
such that
\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha_{\bar{S}}=\rho
and such that the pullback of
\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}}
under
\alpha
is equal to
\bar{\eta}_{X}
. Note that the requirement on
\rho
in
(2)
above implies that

2\mathrm{height}(\rho)=\mathrm{height}(p^{i}\mid X)=4di.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
. We define the formal scheme
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
by

(5.2.4)

\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i).

Then
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
parametrizes tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
such that
\rho
respects the polarization
\lambda_{\bar{X}}
on
\bar{X}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}
up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
, and a CL level structure on
X
.
We define a map

(5.2.5)

\mathfrak{v}_{r}:{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}

sending the tuple
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
to
(2d)^{-1}\mathrm{height}(\rho)
and hence
\mathfrak{v}_{r}({\mathcal{M}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i))=i
.

The group
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts naturally on
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
via the framing
\rho
by
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{G}
, the group
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts on the tower of formal schemes
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}

The same formula as (
5.1.9
) defines a Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}
down to
O_{E_{r}}
. We denote by
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}
the formal scheme
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{r}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
, with its Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}
down to
O_{E}
.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

There exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{E}}

\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}

which is equivariant with respect to the action of
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{G}
, the isomorphism is equivariant for the natural Hecke actions of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

This isomorphism is compatible with the Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}
down to
O_{E}
on the left hand side and the Weil descent datum on the right hand side given by multiplication by the special element
\widetilde{w}
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
for our fixed uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
, viewed as an element in the center of
{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

This statement is to be compared with
[
20
, Corollaries 6.3.4, 6.4.5, 6.5.3]
. In loc. cit., the Weil descent data of
\mathcal{M}_{r}
down to
O_{E_{r}}
is not explicit. The key point here is that we can make explicit the Weil descent datum down to
O_{E}
. To prove Proposition
5.2.3
, we need to go back to
[
20
, §6]
.

Let
S
be a scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
. Given a point
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})\in{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(S)
, we consider the tuple
(C,\iota,\phi,\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
obtained by applying to it the polarized contraction functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r}^{\rm pol}

[
20
, Theorem 4.5.11]
. Let
(\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\phi_{\mathbb{X}})
be the image of
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
under
\mathfrak{C}_{r}^{\rm pol}
. Here
\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}
is a constant
p
-adic étale sheaf on
\Spec W(\bar{\kappa})
associated to a module
C_{\mathbb{X}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-rank
4d
equipped with an action
\iota_{\mathbb{X}}:O_{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(C_{\mathbb{X}})
, and
\phi_{\mathbb{X}}:C_{\mathbb{X}}\times C_{\mathbb{X}}\rightarrow O_{F}
is an
O_{F}
-linear alternating form such that
\phi_{\mathbb{X}}(\iota(a)c_{1},c_{2})=\phi(c_{1},\iota(\bar{a})c_{2})
for

c_{1},c_{2}\in C_{\mathbb{X}}
and
a\in O_{K}
and such that
\phi_{\mathbb{X}}
is perfect unless
K/F
is unramified and
{\mathrm{inv}}(V)=-1
in which case
\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}(\mathrm{det}(\phi_{\mathbb{X}}))=2
.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we consider the functor
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i)
on the category of schemes
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}
. A point of
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i)
is given
by the following data:

(1)

A locally
constant
p
-adic étale sheaf
C
on
S
which is

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-flat with
\rank_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C=4d
and with an action

\iota:O_{K}\longrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C;

(2)

An alternating
O_{F}
-bilinear pairing

\phi:C\times C\longrightarrow O_{F},

such that
\phi(\iota(a)c_{1},c_{2})=\phi(c_{1},\iota(\bar{a})c_{2})
for

c_{1},c_{2}\in C
and
a\in O_{K}
and such that
\phi
is perfect unless
K/F
is unramified and
{\mathrm{inv}}(V)=-1
in which case
\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}(\mathrm{det}(\phi))=2
;

(3)

A quasi-isogeny of
O_{K}
-module sheaves on
S

\rho:(C,\iota)\longrightarrow(\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}},\iota_{\mathbb{X}})

such that
\rho
respects the bilinear form
p^{i}\phi
on
C
and the bilinear form
\phi_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\underline{C}_{\mathbb{\mathbb{X}}}
up to unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
;

(4)

A class
\bar{\eta}_{C}
of isomorphisms of
p
-adic étale sheaves on
\bar{S}

{\eta}_{C}:(C,\xi)(\Lambda,\varsigma)\;\mathrm{mod}\;\mathbf{K}_{G}

which respect the bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Here we define the alternating form
\xi:C\times C\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
by
\xi:=\mathrm{Tr}_{F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\vartheta^{-1}\phi
where
\vartheta
is the different of the extension
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(C,\iota,\phi,\rho,\bar{\eta}_{C})
and refer to the datum
(4)
as a (relaxed) CL-level structure on
(C,\iota,\phi,\rho)
. Another set of data
(C^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{C^{\prime}})
defines the same point
iff there is an isomorphism
\alpha:(C,\iota)(C^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
such that

\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha=\rho
and such that the pullback of
\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{C^{\prime}}
under
\alpha
is equal to
\bar{\eta}_{C}
. Then
\alpha
respects
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
up
to a factor in
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
.
We set

(5.2.6)

\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}=\coprod_{i}\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(i).

Proof of Proposition
5.2.3
.

The polarized contraction functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r}^{\rm pol}
of
[
20
, Theorem 4.5.11]
gives an isomorphism of functors between
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
(here we have used
[
20
, Proposition 7.4.7]
for the equivalence of CL-level structures on both sides). On the other hand, the proof of
[
20
, (7.4.11)]
shows that there is a natural isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}

\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\simeq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}.

Combining the two isomorphisms, we obtain an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r}}

\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\simeq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G},

which is equivariant for the action of
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{G}
, this isomorphism is by construction equivariant for the natural Hecke actions of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

Next we explain the compatibility of Weil descent data down to
O_{E}
. Let
S=\Spec R
be an affine scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
and
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})
be a point on
\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})
for some integer
i
. Let the quasi-isogeny

(5.2.7)

X_{\bar{R}}\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}}\tau_{E\ast}\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}

correspond via the contraction functor
\mathfrak{C}_{r}^{\rm pol}
to the quasi-isogeny

C\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}}\tau_{E\ast}\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}\simeq\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}

for a tuple
(C,\iota,\phi,\rho:C\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{X}},\bar{\eta}_{C})
in
\mathcal{G}_{r}(\bar{R})
. We want to make the map
\rho F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}\circ\rho
explicit.

We recall the following notation from the beginning of §
3.1
.
In the case where
K/F
is a ramified extension of local fields
(ramified case) we choose a prime element
\Pi\in O_{K}

such that

\bar{\Pi}=-\Pi
. Then
\pi=-\Pi^{2}
is a prime element
of
F
. In the case where
K/F
is an unramified extension of local
fields (unramified case) or where
K\cong F\times F
(split case) we
choose a prime element
\pi\in F
and we set
\Pi=\pi
.

The map sending the quasi-isogeny
C\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}
to the composite

C\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}\xrightarrow{F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E}}}\underline{C}_{\mathbb{X}}

corresponds to the map on
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}
given

(1)

when
K/F
is ramified, by

\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})\xrightarrow{\Pi^{ef_{E}}}\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i+ef_{E})(\bar{R}),

cf.
[
20
, Lemma 6.3.3]
;

(2)

when
K/F
is unramified, by

\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})\xrightarrow{\pi^{ef_{E}/2}}\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i+ef_{E})(\bar{R}),

cf.
[
20
, Lemma 6.4.4]
;

(3)

when
K/F
is split, by

\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})\xrightarrow{(\pi^{a_{1,E}},\pi^{a_{2,E}})}\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i+ef_{E})(\bar{R}),

cf.
[
20
, Lemma 6.5.2]
. Here
2a_{i}=\dim\mathbb{X}_{i}
and
fa_{i,E}=f_{E}a_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.

Here these maps over the arrows are the natural translation maps on the moduli space
\mathcal{G}_{r,O_{\breve{E}}}
given by multiplication by the corresponding element of
K
. More precisely, in loc. cit., the Weil descent datum down to
O_{E_{r}}
is given. We use that the elements for the Weil descent datum down to
O_{E}
are obtained from these elements by raising them to the
f_{E}/f_{E_{r}}
-th power (for the split case, use the identity
fa_{i,E_{r}}=f_{E_{r}}a_{i}
, cf.
[
20
, below (6.5.1)]
).
We denote these elements by
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
. By
[
20
, Remark 7.2.6]
, we can modify the element
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
by a unit in
O_{K}
without affecting the conclusion. Therefore, we can replace the element
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
by the element
\widetilde{w}
appearing in the statement of the proposition. Indeed,
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
has norm
\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w}^{\prime})=\pi^{ef_{E}}
and
\widetilde{w}
has norm
\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w})=up^{f_{E}}
for some unit
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. The proposition follows.
∎

We have a morphism of formal moduli spaces over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}

(5.2.8)

\vartheta:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}},

sending
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})
to
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})\times(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
.
Here we define the relaxed CL-level structure
\bar{\eta}_{X}
as follows. Given
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
, we can associate to it a unique class of isomorphisms of
p
-adic étale sheaves
\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{X_{0}}\mod\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
which respect the natural bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(one should regard this as a CL-level structure on
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
). To see this, recall from
[
20
, (7.4.14)]
that
C_{\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}}=O_{K}
. Consider the moduli problem of
{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}
in §
4
. The proof of Proposition
4.2.5
shows that there is a unique
g\in\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
such that there is an isomorphism
(gC_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}},(p^{i})^{-1}\xi_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}})\simeq(C_{X_{0}},\xi_{X_{0}})
which respects the bilinear forms up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, where
i
is determined by
\xi_{\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}}(gc_{1},gc_{2})=p^{i}\xi_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}(c_{1},c_{2})
for
c_{1},c_{2}
in
C_{\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}}
. Since multiplication by
g
gives an isomorphism
(C_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}},\xi_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}})\simeq(gC_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}},(p^{i})^{-1}\xi_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}})
, the composition of the above two isomorphisms give us the desired isomorphism

\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{X_{0}}\mod\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
which respects the natural bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit
u
in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We note that this unit is the same as the one appearing in datum
(2)
of Definition
4.2.3
.

Given an isomorphism of
p
-adic étale sheaves

\eta:(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X),\mathfrak{e})(\Lambda,\varsigma)

which strictly respects the bilinear forms on both sides, we choose an isomorphism
\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{X_{0}}
in the above class to obtain an isomorphism

\eta_{X}:(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0},X),\mathfrak{e}_{X})(\Lambda,\varsigma)

where
\mathfrak{e}_{X}
is defined similarly as
\mathfrak{e}
. Here we use the fully faithfulness of the polarized contraction functor. This isomorphism respects the bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and its class under the action of
\mathbf{K}_{G}
gives the desired relaxed CL-level structure on
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
. One easily checks that choosing a different
\eta_{0}
gives an isomorphism
\eta_{X}
in the same
\mathbf{K}_{G}
-orbit as the original one. This defines
\vartheta
.

Conversely, if we are given an isomorphism

\eta_{X}:(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0},X),\mathfrak{e}_{X})(\Lambda,\varsigma)

which respects the bilinear forms on both sides up a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and an isomorphism
\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{X_{0}}
which respects the bilinear forms on both sides up to the same unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, then we can reverse the above procedure to obtain a strict CL-level structure on
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})
. Indeed,
(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0},X),\mathfrak{e}_{X})
can be identified with
(C_{X},\xi_{X})
therefore we obtain an isomorphism
\eta_{X}:(C_{X},\xi_{X})\simeq(\Lambda,\varsigma)
which respects the bilinear forms on both sides up to a unit
u_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. This unit is the same unit as the one in datum
(2)
of Definition
5.2.2
. Now using the isomorphism
\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}}C_{X_{0}}
, we can still identify
\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X)
with
C_{X}
but the bilinear form
\mathfrak{e}
is identified with
\xi_{X}
up to the inverse of the unit
u_{2}
that comes from comparing the bilinear forms under
\eta_{0}:C_{\widetilde{}\mathbb{X}_{0}}\simeq C_{X_{0}}
. We note that again
u_{2}
is the same unit as the one appearing in datum
(2)
of Definition
4.2.3
. Therefore under the isomorphism
(\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(X_{0},X),\mathfrak{e})\simeq(\Lambda,\varsigma)
the two bilinear forms differ by
u^{-1}_{2}u_{1}
. In order to obtain a strict CL-level structure, we need to have
u_{1}=u_{2}
. In this case, we will say
\eta_{X}
and
\eta_{0}
are compatible. Suppose now that
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})
lies on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)
for some
i
. Then
\eta_{X}
and
\eta_{0}
are compatible by definition of the moduli problem, see datum
(2)
and
(4)
of Definition
5.2.1
.

The above discussion and Remark
5.1.2
show that (
5.2.8
) is a closed immersion of formal schemes, equivariant with respect to the action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
and that this closed immersion is compatible with the natural Weil descent data down to
O_{E}
on both sides. In fact, it shows that (
5.2.8
) induces an isomorphism

\vartheta:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i),\quad i\in\mathbb{Z},

since the image of
\vartheta
consists of those tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}_{X})\times(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0})
such that
\eta_{X}
and
\eta_{0}
are compatible which is automatically true by the above discussion.

We equip the right-hand side of (
5.2.8
) with the natural descent datum given by
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}
and with an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via the map
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proposition 5.2.4
.

There is an isomorphism of
O_{\breve{E}}
-formal schemes

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},

which is compatible with natural actions of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides. For varying
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
, the isomorphism is equivariant for the natural Hecke actions of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

The Weil descent datum
\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}}

on the left-hand side corresponds on the right-hand side to the Weil descent datum down to
O_{E}
given by multiplication by the special element
\widetilde{w}
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E)
for our fixed uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
, viewed as an element in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proof.

We have

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i),\quad i\in\mathbb{Z}.

By Proposition
5.2.3
and Remark
5.1.2
, we therefore obtain the desired isomorphism.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}

\displaystyle=\coprod_{i}\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}.

For varying
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
, the isomorphism is equivariant for the natural Hecke actions of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides by construction and Proposition
5.2.3
.

Let
\Spec R
be a scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
. From the compatibility of Weil descent data of the closed immersion
\vartheta
, we obtain the commutativity of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i)\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i)(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G},O_{\breve{E}}}(i+f_{E})\times\mathcal{M}_{0,O_{\breve{E}}}(i+f_{E})(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Thus we have the commutativity of the following diagram

(5.2.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R}_{[\tau_{E}]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

But by Proposition
4.2.5
(via Remark
4.2.2
) and Proposition
5.2.3
,
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r},E}\times\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{0},E}
is given by multiplication by
(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w})
on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
. Under the isomorphism
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
, this is given simply by multiplication by
\widetilde{w}
.
∎

6.
p
-adic uniformization of RSZ Shimura curves

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.3.3
. We fix the notation as in that theorem: we therefore have the CM-extension
K/F
, the fixed embedding
\varphi_{0}:K\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
, the CM-type
r
of rank
2
special relative to the restriction
w_{0}
of
\varphi_{0}
to
F
, with its reflex field
E_{r}
; we also have a CM-type
r_{0}
of rank
1
with its associated classical CM-type
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}_{0}
for which
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi_{0}^{+}
and the composite
E=E_{\Phi^{+}_{0}}E_{r}=E_{0}E_{r}
. We further have the prime number
p
, a fixed embedding
\nu:\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, the induced place
\nu
of
E
and the induced place
v_{0}
of
F
via the embedding
{\varphi_{0}}_{|_{F}}:FE
. We denote by
\kappa_{\nu}:=\kappa_{E_{\nu}}
the residue field of
E_{\nu}
whose cardinality is
p^{f_{E_{\nu}}}
, where
f_{E_{\nu}}
is the inertia index. We fix a uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}:=\varpi_{E_{\nu}}
of the ring of integers
O_{E_{\nu}}
of
E_{\nu}
.

We also have the
K/F
-anti-hermitian vector space
(V,\varkappa)
of signature
r
; it is assumed that
v_{0}
does not split in
K
and that
V_{v_{0}}
is non-split. We will refer to
v_{0}
as the special prime, the other
p
-adic places
v
of
F
will be called the banal primes. Finally, we fix an almost selfdual lattice
\Lambda_{v}\subset V_{v}=V\otimes_{F}F_{v}
for each
p
-adic place
v
of
F
in the sense of the Notation section at the end of the Introduction.

We will only consider compact open subgroups
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which, in terms of the product decomposition (
2.1.9
), are of the form

(6.0.1)

\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}},

where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a open compact subgroup of the form

{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}},

where
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{p}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
and
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Then we assume that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}
further decomposes as
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}\cdot\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}
, where
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}
is an open compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We assume that
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
is a maximal open compact and therefore
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}=\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, as follows from our assumption that
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a compact group. Let
{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}={\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G}}={\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
and
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G},p}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{v_{0}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},p}
.

Let
(X,\iota,\lambda)
be a semi-local CM-triple, cf. §
3.4
. We say
(X,\iota,\lambda)=\prod_{v\mid p}(X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{v})
is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized if each local CM-triple
(X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{v})
is
(V_{v},\varsigma_{v})
-principally polarized. Sometimes, we also simply say that
\lambda
is a
(V,\varsigma)
-principal polarization.

6.1.
Semi-local formal moduli space of RSZ CM-triples

We will need a semi-local version of the notion of a special element from Construction
4.2.1
.

Construction 6.1.1
.

Let
\Phi^{+}
be a classical CM-type. Let
E\subset\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
be a finite extension of
E_{\Phi^{+}}
and
\nu:\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be a fixed embedding. Then
\Phi^{+}
defines a homomorphism
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,E}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{E}
and hence by base change

\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,E_{\nu}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{E_{\nu}}.

We obtain the semi-local reflex norm map which is the composition

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E_{\nu}}:\Res_{E_{\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,E_{\nu}})\overset{\mu}{\longrightarrow}\Res_{E_{\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{E_{\nu}})\overset{\Nm_{E_{\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}{\longrightarrow}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}.

Then the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
is given by

(6.1.1)

\widetilde{w}=\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E_{\nu}}(\varpi_{\nu})\in Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Its image in
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is independent of
\varpi_{\nu}
.

Using the embedding
\nu:\bar{}\mathbb{Q}\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have the decomposition
\Phi^{+}=\coprod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\Phi_{v}^{+}
, cf. (
3.4.2
). The local reflex field
E(v)
for
\Phi_{v}^{+}
is contained in
E_{\nu}
.
Under the map

(6.1.2)

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\big(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\big),

the image of
\widetilde{w}
coincides with
({\widetilde{w}}_{v})
, where
\widetilde{w}_{v}
is the special element in
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{v},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
.

Consider the integral Shimura variety
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
in Definition
2.3.2
and consider it as a scheme over
O_{E_{\nu}}
. We fix a point

(6.1.3)

(\bm{A}_{0},\bm{\iota}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{A},\bm{\iota},\bm{\lambda},\bar{\bm{\eta}}^{p},\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{p}^{v_{0}})\in\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{\kappa}_{\nu}).

We denote by
\mathbb{X}_{0}
, resp.
\mathbb{X}
, the
p
-divisible group of
\bm{A}_{0}
, resp.
\bm{A}
. These are equipped with an action
\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
, resp.
\iota_{\mathbb{X}}
, of
O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
induced by
\bm{\iota}_{0}
, resp.
\bm{\iota}
. They are also equipped with a polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
, resp.
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
induced by
\bm{\lambda}_{0}
and
\bm{\lambda}
. Here, because the ideal
\mathfrak{a}
from §
2.2
is prime to
p
, the polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
is principal and
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
is a
(V,\varsigma)
-principal polarization.

In analogy with (
5.1.3
), we set

(6.1.4)

\displaystyle J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{X}_{0}):\alpha^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=\mu_{0}(\alpha){\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}},\;\mu_{0}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\};

\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{X}):\alpha^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})={\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}\};

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{X}):\alpha^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=\mu(\alpha){\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}},\;\mu(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\};

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times J_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Then
J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\mid p}J_{\mathrm{U},v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where
J_{\mathrm{U},v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{\alpha_{v}\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K_{v}}(\mathbb{X}_{v}):\alpha^{\ast}_{v}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})={\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{v}}\}
.

For
v\neq v_{0}
,
J_{\mathrm{U},v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{U}(V_{v})(F_{v})
and
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{U}(\bar{V}_{v_{0}})(F_{v_{0}})
, cf.
[
20
, (7.2.6), (7.2.8)]
. We define

(6.1.5)

\displaystyle J^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod\nolimits_{v\neq v_{0},v\mid p}J_{\mathrm{U},v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=J^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod\nolimits_{v\neq v_{0},v\mid p}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Then we have an isomorphism
\widetilde{J}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For each
v\mid p
, we set

(6.1.6)

\widetilde{G}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Definition 6.1.2
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
a functor
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i)
on
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
. Let
S
be a scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
. A point of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i)(S)
is given by the following data:

(1)

A CM-triple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
of type
(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r_{0})
satisfying the condition
({\rm KC}_{r_{0}})
;

(2)

An
O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:\bar{X}_{0}:=X_{0}\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{0}\times_{\Spec k}\bar{S}

such that locally on
\bar{S}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
such that
\rho^{\ast}_{0,v}({\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0,v}})=up^{i}\lambda_{0,v}
for each
v\mid p
;

(3)

A CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
of type

(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r)
over
S

which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized.

(4)

An
O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
\bar{S}
,
\rho^{\ast}_{v}({\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})=up^{i}\lambda_{\bar{X}_{v}}
for each
v\mid p
, with the same
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
as in datum
(2)
;

(5)

A class
\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}}
of isomorphisms of étale sheaves

\eta_{p}^{v_{0}}:\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K^{v_{0}}_{p}}}(X_{0}^{v_{0}},X^{v_{0}})\Lambda\otimes_{O_{K}}O_{K^{v_{0}}_{p}}\;\mathrm{mod}\;\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}

which respect the bilinear forms on
\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K_{v}}}(X_{0,v},X_{v})
and
\Lambda\otimes_{O_{K}}O_{K_{v}}
for each
p
-adic place
v\neq v_{0}
.

We denote these data by a tuple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
. Two tuples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
and
(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\rho^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda_{X^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime})
define the same point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i)
iff there exist isomorphisms
\alpha_{0}:(X_{0},\iota_{0})(X^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{0})
and
\alpha:(X,\iota)(X^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
of
O_{K}
-modules such that
\rho^{\prime}_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,\bar{S}}=\rho_{0}
and
\rho^{\prime}\circ\alpha_{\bar{S}}=\rho
and such that
(\alpha,\alpha_{0})
pulls back
\bar{\eta}_{p}^{\prime v_{0}}
to
\bar{\eta}_{p}^{v_{0}}
.

Remark 6.1.3
.

We could replace the condition in
(2)
by
\rho_{0}^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{0}})=p^{i}{\lambda}_{\bar{X}_{0}}
and the condition in
(4)
by
\rho^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=p^{i}\lambda_{\bar{X}}
without changing the formal moduli space. Similarly, if
p^{i}
is replaced by
up^{i}
with
u\in O_{F}^{\times}
in both equations, the formal moduli space will not change. This follows from the same reasoning as in
[
20
, Remark 7.2.3]
.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i)
. We define the formal scheme

(6.1.7)

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}=\bigsqcup\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i).

Thus
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
parametrizes
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
such that
\rho_{0}
respects the polarization
\lambda_{0}
on
\bar{X}_{0}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}
on
\mathbb{X}_{0}
up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
and such that
\rho
respects the polarization
\lambda_{X}
on
\bar{X}
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}
up to the same factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
. Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
affords an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We refer to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
as the semi-local formal moduli space of RSZ CM-triples. Following the same procedure as in (
5.1.6
), we define a Weil descent datum

(6.1.8)

\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(\tau_{E_{\nu}})}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}

where the upper index
(\tau_{E_{\nu}})
denotes the base change via

\Spec\tau_{E_{\nu}}:\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\longrightarrow\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
.

We fix an isomorphism
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Via this identification, we then obtain an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\hat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}
via
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Note that
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}\simeq\mathrm{U}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U}}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},p}
since
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
. Then we obtain an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}
via the factor
\widetilde{J}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proposition 6.1.4
.

There exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\hat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}

which is equivariant with respect to the action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides. For varying
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G},p}
, this isomorphism is compatible with Hecke action of
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on both sides.

The Weil descent datum
\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}}
down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on the left-hand side corresponds on the right-hand side to the Weil descent datum given by

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}g),\quad g\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Here
\widetilde{w}
is the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
, as in Construction
6.1.1
, considered as an element in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proof.

Let
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)
be a tuple parametrized by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
. Then the decomposition

X_{0}=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}X_{0,v},\quad X=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}X_{v}

extends to the tuple

(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho)=\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}(X_{0,v},\iota_{0,v},\lambda_{0,v},\rho_{0,v},X_{v},\iota_{v},\lambda_{X_{v}},\rho_{v},\bar{\eta}_{v}).

This in turn gives rise to a map

(6.1.9)

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\longrightarrow\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v}}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}(v)}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}.

Here
\breve{E}(v)
is the completion of the maximal unramified extension of the composite
E(v)
of the local reflex fields for
r_{v}
and
r_{0,v}
(recall that
\nu
defines the decomposition
\Phi=\bigsqcup_{v}\Phi_{v}
and the restrictions
r_{v}
and
r_{0,v}
of
r
and
r_{0}
). Then
\breve{E}(v)\subset\breve{E}_{\nu}
. Furthermore,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v_{0}}}
is given by the formal scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
defined in (
5.1.2
); for
v\neq v_{0}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v}}
is given by the formal scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
defined in Definition (
5.2.1
).

The map (
6.1.9
) is an isomorphism. Indeed, comparing the moduli descriptions, we see that the RHS is characterized by the fact that the compatibility scalars in 2) and 4) are the same for any
v\mid p
, whereas the LHS is characterized by the fact that the compatibility scalars in 2) and 4) are the same for
all

v\mid p
. But appealing, as in Remark
5.1.2
to
[
20
, Remark 7.2.3]
, we see that in passing to isomorphism classes, the difference between these conditions is irrelevant. The Hecke equivariance of (
6.1.9
) is obvious.
Now the claimed isomorphism over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
follows from Proposition
5.1.6
and Proposition
5.2.4
.

The Weil descent datum on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v_{0}}}
from
O_{\breve{E}({v_{0}})}
down to
O_{E({v_{0}})}
is given under the isomorphism with
(\hat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}({v_{0}})})\times\widetilde{G}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v_{0}}
of Proposition
5.1.6
by

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E({v_{0}})}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}_{v_{0}}g),\quad g\in\widetilde{G}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},v_{0}},

where
\widetilde{w}_{v_{0}}
is the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0,v_{0}},E({v_{0})})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{E({v_{0}})}
, as in Construction
4.2.1
. A similar formula follows from Proposition
5.2.4
for
v\neq v_{0}
. The result follows now from the compatibility (
6.1.2
) of
\widetilde{w}
with localization and Remark
4.2.2
on the behavior of special elements under passage to bigger fields (applied to
E_{\nu}\supset E(v)
).

∎

6.2.
p
-adic uniformization

Recall from (
6.1.3
) the base point
