(\bm{A}_{0},\bm{\iota}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{A},\bm{\iota},\bm{\lambda},\bar{\bm{\eta}}^{p},\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{p}^{v_{0}})\in\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{\kappa}_{\nu})
. We define the algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbf{A}_{0}):\alpha^{\ast}(\bm{\lambda}_{0})=\mu_{0}(\alpha)\bm{\lambda}_{0},\;\mu_{0}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\};

\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbf{A}):\alpha^{\ast}(\bm{\lambda})=\bm{\lambda}\};

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in\mathrm{Aut}^{\circ}_{K}(\mathbf{A}):\alpha^{\ast}(\bm{\lambda})=\mu(\alpha)\bm{\lambda},\;\mu(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\};

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=J(\mathbb{Q})\times_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q})=J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q}).

The Dieudonné modules of
\mathbf{A}_{0}
and
\mathbf{A}
are isoclinic (for
\mathbf{A}_{0}
this is obvious, for
\mathbf{A}
, this follows from
[
20
, Proposition 7.1.11]
). This implies that the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued points
J_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
coincide with the groups (
6.1.4
), cf.
[
25
, Cor. 6.29]
.

Now we can prove the main
p
-adic uniformization theorem for the RSZ Shimura curve introduced in Theorem
2.3.3
.

Theorem 6.2.1
.

Let
(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}
be the formal completion of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
along its special fiber, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}
. Then there exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
,

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big].

The quotient on the right-hand-side is defined via the composition

\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

using an identification of the adjoint group
{J}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
and through an action of

\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}
. For varying
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G}}
, the isomorphism is equivariant for the action of the group
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which acts on both sides by Hecke correspondences.

Furthermore, the natural descent datum down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on the left-hand side is given on the right-hand side by

(\xi,h)\rightarrow(\omega_{\omega_{\Omega,E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}h),\quad h\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),

where
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
, as in Construction
6.1.1
, considered as an element in the center of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

We define as follows the uniformization morphism

(6.2.2)

\Theta:\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\widetilde{G}}\longrightarrow(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}.

Let
{S}=\Spec{R}
, and let
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\rho,\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})
be a point of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}(i)
over
{R}
and let

g\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. The quasi-isogeny
\rho
over
\bar{R}=R\otimes_{O_{E_{\nu}}}\bar{\kappa}_{\nu}

extends uniquely to a quasi-isogeny of abelian varieties

\rho:\bar{A}\longrightarrow\bm{A}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}_{{\nu}}}\Spec\bar{R}.

Because
O_{K}
acts on
\bar{X}=X\otimes_{R}\bar{R}
, we obtain a map

O_{K}\End(\bar{A})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Moreover, the polarization

\bm{\lambda}:\bm{A}\longrightarrow\bm{A}^{\vee}

induces on
\bar{A}
a quasi-polarization

\lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}:\bar{A}\longrightarrow\bar{A}^{\vee}
.
On the
p
-divisible groups,
\lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}
is given by
p^{i}\lambda
and therefore

\lambda_{\bar{A}}:=p^{-i}\lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}
satisfies the condition (ii)
in the Definition
2.3.2
of the functor
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
. Similarly, we obtain
(\bar{A}_{0},\iota_{\bar{A}_{0}},\lambda_{\bar{A}_{0}})
and the quasi-isogeny

\rho_{0}:\bar{A}_{0}\longrightarrow\bm{A}_{0}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}_{{\nu}}}\Spec\bar{R}.

The quasi-isogenies
\rho_{0}
and
\rho
induce an isomorphism
\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\bar{A}_{0},\bar{A})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{p}({\bm{A}}_{0},{\bm{A}})
given by
x\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\rho)^{-1}\circ x\circ\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\rho_{0})
. Hence
\bm{\bar{\eta}}^{p}
induces an isomorphism

{\eta}^{p}:\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\bar{A}_{0},\bar{A})\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p}\;\mod{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}.

The same construction furnishes the isomorphism

{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p}:\widehat{\mathrm{V}}^{v_{0}}_{p}(\bar{A}_{0},\bar{A})\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}K^{v_{0}}_{p}\;\mod{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}.

We associate to the tuple
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\rho,\bar{\eta}^{v}_{p},g)
from the left-hand side of (
6.2.2
)
the point

(6.2.3)

(\bar{A}_{0},\iota_{\bar{A}_{0}},\lambda_{\bar{A}_{0}},\bar{A},\iota_{\bar{A}},\lambda_{\bar{A}},\bar{\eta}^{p}g,\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R}).

The semi-local CM-triples
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0})
and
(X,\iota,\lambda)
over
R
define by the Serre-Tate theorem
a lifting of (
6.2.3
) to a point of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(R)
. The map is functorial in
R
and defines
the uniformization morphism
\Theta
in
(
6.2.2
).

Recall
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
from (
6.2.1
). We define an action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
on the left hand side of (
6.2.2
).
Let
\widetilde{\alpha}=({\alpha}_{0},\alpha)\in\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
, then we define
\omega(\widetilde{\alpha})\in\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
by the equation

(6.2.4)

\bm{\eta}^{p}\circ{\widehat{}\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\alpha^{-1})\circ{\widehat{}\mathrm{V}}^{p}(\alpha_{0})=\omega(\widetilde{\alpha})\circ\bm{\eta}^{p}.

This defines a homomorphism

\omega:\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f}).

We already noted that
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
coincides with (
6.1.4
), and hence acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
by

\widetilde{\alpha}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha):(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta})(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho,\bar{\eta}).

Let
((X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}),g)
be a point of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
over
\bar{S}
and let

(\bar{A}_{0},\iota_{\bar{A}_{0}},\lambda_{\bar{A}_{0}},\bar{A},\iota_{\bar{A}},\lambda_{\bar{A}},g\bar{\eta}^{p},\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R})

be its image under
\Theta
. For
\widetilde{\alpha}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha)\in\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the quasi-isogenies
\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0}
and
\alpha\circ\rho
extend to quasi-isogenies of abelian varieties

\bar{A}_{0}\overset{\rho_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\bm{A}_{0,\bar{R}}\overset{\alpha_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\bm{A}_{0,\bar{R}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{A}\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\bm{A}_{\bar{R}}\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}\bm{A}_{\bar{R}}

The image of
((X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho,\bar{\eta}),g)
under the map
\Theta
is given by

(\bar{A}_{0},\iota_{\bar{A}_{0}},\lambda_{\bar{A}_{0}},\bar{A},\iota_{\bar{A}},\lambda_{\bar{A}},\omega({\widetilde{\alpha}}^{-1})g\bar{\eta}^{p},\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{R}).

Hence, if we define the action of
\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})
on the source of
\Theta
by

((X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\rho,\bar{\eta}),g)((X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0}\circ\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda_{X},\alpha\circ\rho,\bar{\eta}),\omega(\widetilde{\alpha})g),

then
\Theta
is equivariant. The uniformization theorem for PEL-type Shimura varieties
[
25
, Theorem 6.2.3]
shows now that
\Theta
induces an isomorphism

\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\widetilde{G}}\big]\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{{\rm Spf}\,O_{E_{\nu}}}{\rm Spf}\,O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}.

Note here we are using the fact that
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\bar{\kappa}_{\nu})
has only one isogeny class, as follows from
[
20
, Proposition 7.11]
. By Proposition
6.1.4
, we can rewrite the left hand side of this isomorphism as

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[(\hat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\widetilde{G}}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[(\hat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big].

The Hecke equivariance statement follows from that of
[
25
, Theorem 6.2.3]
and Proposition
6.1.4
.

Finally we prove that the natural Weil descent data on the two sides of (
6.2.2
) are compatible. This will conclude the proof. The Weil descent datum
\omega_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}}
is obtained by changing
\rho_{0}
to
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
and
\rho
to
\widetilde{\rho}
where

\widetilde{\rho}_{0}:X_{0}\overset{\rho_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0}\overset{\varepsilon_{\ast}F_{\mathbb{X}_{0},\tau_{E_{\nu}}}}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}(\tau_{E_{\nu}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}_{0},\quad\text{ resp.}\quad\widetilde{\rho}:X\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}\mathbb{X}\overset{\varepsilon_{\ast}F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{\nu}}}}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}(\tau_{E_{\nu}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}.

This gives a point
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\widetilde{\rho},\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G},p}}
. The point
(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\rho,\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})
defines a quasi-isogeny of abelian varieties

\rho_{0}:A_{0}\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{\ast}\bm{A}_{0},\quad\text{ resp.}\quad\rho:A\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{\ast}\bm{A},

as explained in the definition of
\Theta
. The point

(X_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},X,\iota,\lambda,\widetilde{\rho},\bar{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p})
defines in the same way the quasi-isogeny of
abelian varieties over
R_{[\tau_{E_{\nu}}]}
,

(6.2.5)

{A}_{0}\overset{\rho_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}\bm{A}_{0}\overset{\varepsilon_{\ast}F_{\bm{A}_{0},\tau_{E_{\nu}}}}{\longrightarrow}(\varepsilon\tau_{E_{\nu}})_{\ast}\bm{A}_{0},\quad\text{ resp.}\quad A\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\varepsilon_{\ast}\bm{A}\overset{\varepsilon_{\ast}F_{\bm{A},\tau_{E_{\nu}}}}{\longrightarrow}(\varepsilon\tau_{E_{\nu}})_{\ast}\bm{A}.

Here
A_{0}
and
A
with their additional structure are regarded as points of

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}(R_{[\tau_{E_{\nu}}]})
. This makes sense because to be a
point of
(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}(R)
depends only on the

\kappa_{{\nu}}
-algebra structure on
R
. In other words

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})(R)=(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})(R_{[\tau_{E_{\nu}}]}).

But (
6.2.5
) is exactly how the Weil descent datum on the right hand side of (
6.2.2
) is defined.
∎

Corollary 6.2.2
.

Assume that
\mathbf{K}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}
is sufficiently small, then the functor
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
is representable by a projective flat
O_{E,(\nu)}
-scheme
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
which is a stable relative curve.

Proof.

This follows from the same argument as in the proof of
[
20
, Theorem 7.3.3]
.
∎

Corollary 6.2.3
.

There exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
,

(
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}\big]\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

The quotient in the first factor on the right-hand-side is defined using an identification of the adjoint group
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
and through an action of
J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}
. For varying
\mathbf{K}^{v_{0}}_{\widetilde{G}}
, the isomorphism is equivariant for the action of the group
\widetilde{G}^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which acts on both sides by Hecke correspondences.

Furthermore, the natural descent datum down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on the left-hand side is given on the right-hand side by

(\xi,h,z)\rightarrow(\omega_{\omega_{\Omega,E_{\nu}}}(\xi),h,\widetilde{w}z),\quad h\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\quad z\in Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})

where
\widetilde{w}\in Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the special element relative to
(\Phi^{+}_{0},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
, as in Construction
6.1.1
.

Proof.

This follows from the previous theorem by using the decomposition
\widetilde{J}=J_{\mathrm{U}}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\widetilde{G}=\mathrm{U}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
.
∎

Part III
KRZ Shimura curves revisited

7.
p
-adic uniformization of KRZ Shimura curves revisited

Let
K/F
be a CM field. Let
r
be a CM-type of rank
2
special relative to the archimedean place
w_{0}
of
F
whose extension to
K
corresponds to two embeddings
\varphi_{0}
and
\bar{\varphi}_{0}
of
K
in
\mathbb{C}
. The reflex field of
r
will be denoted by
E_{r}
.

Let
p
be a prime number and
\nu:\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be an embedding. The
p
-adic place which is induced on any subfield of
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
will always be denoted by
\nu
. We fix a uniformizer
\varpi_{r,\nu}:=\varpi_{E_{r,\nu}}
of
E_{r,\nu}
and denote by
\kappa_{r,\nu}:=\kappa_{E_{r,\nu}}
the residual class field of
E_{r,\nu}
, and by
f_{E_{r,\nu}}
the inertia index. We denote by
v_{0}
the
p
-adic place of
F
induced by

F\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}E_{r}\xrightarrow{\nu}\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

which we will refer to as the special prime; the other
p
-adic places
v
of
F
will be called the banal primes. It is assumed that
v_{0}
does not split in
K
.

7.1.
The KRZ Shimura curve

Let
(V,\varsigma)
be the two-dimensional
K
-vector space considered in (
2.1.1
) and let
G
be the unitary similitude group of
(V,\varsigma)
with rational similitude factor. For each place
v\mid p
in
F
, let
G_{v}
be the group of unitary similitudes of
(V_{v},\varsigma_{v})
with similitude factor in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
. Then
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the subgroup of
\prod_{v\mid p}G_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
where the similitudes of all factors are identical. For each place
v\mid p
in
F
, we fix a lattice
\Lambda_{v}\subset V_{v}
which is almost self-dual with respect to
\varsigma_{v}
(see the Notation section at the end of the Introduction for the meaning of this terminology).

We fix an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{G}
of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
of the form

\mathbf{K}_{G}=\mathbf{K}_{G}^{p}\cdot\mathbf{K}_{G,p}

where
\mathbf{K}_{G}^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}^{p})
and
\mathbf{K}_{G,p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We impose the following assumptions on
\mathbf{K}_{G,p}
, cf.
[
20
, (7.1.8)]
:

\mathbf{K}_{G,p}=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\big(\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\mathbf{K}_{G,v}\big),

where
\mathbf{K}_{G,v}=\{g\in G_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):g\Lambda_{v}=\Lambda_{v}\}
is an open compact subgroup of
G_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Definition 7.1.1
.

We define the category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
fibered in groupoids on
({\rm LNSch})/O_{E_{r},(\nu)}
which associates to each
O_{E_{r},({\nu})}
-scheme
S
the groupoid of tuples
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta}^{p})
consisting of the following data:

(1)

An abelian scheme
A
over
S
, up to isogeny of degree
prime to
p
, with an algebra homomorphism

\iota:O_{K,(p)}\longrightarrow\End_{(p)}(A)

such that
\Lie A\otimes_{O_{E_{r},(\nu)}}O_{E_{r,\nu}}

satisfies the condition

({\rm KC}_{r})
;

(2)

A
\mathbb{Q}
-homogeneous polarization
\bar{\lambda}
of
A

such that the Rosati involution induces the conjugation of
K/F
;

(3)

A class of
O_{K}
-linear isomorphisms

\bar{\eta}^{p}:\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A)\rightarrow V\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\;\text{mod}\;{{\mathbf{K}}}^{p}_{G}

which respect the forms on both sides up to a constant in

\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}(1)^{\times}
(and satisfies the usual Galois invariance property, comp. Definition
2.3.2
).

We impose the following conditions.

(i)

The action of
O_{K,(p)}
on
\Lie A
satisfies the Eisenstein condition
({\rm EC}_{r})

(ii)

There exists a polarization

\lambda\in\bar{\lambda}
such that, for the
p
-primary part of
\ker(\lambda)
we have
\ker(\lambda)_{p}=\prod_{v\mid p}\ker(\lambda)_{v}
, where
\ker(\lambda)_{v}
is trivial unless
v
is unramified in
K
and
{\mathrm{inv}}(V_{v})=-1
, in which case
{\pi_{v}}_{|_{\ker(\lambda)_{v}}}=0
and
\ker(\lambda)_{v}
has rank
\#(\Lambda_{v}^{\vee}/\Lambda_{v})
, comp. Definition
2.3.2
, (ii);

(iii)

There is an identity of invariants,

{\mathrm{inv}}_{v}^{r}(A,\iota,\lambda)={\mathrm{inv}}(V_{v}),\quad\text{ for all }\,v|p,

cf.
[
20
, Definition 7.1.2]
.

An isomorphism of such data
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta}^{p})(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\bar{\lambda}^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime p})
is given by a
O_{K}
-linear quasi-isogeny
\phi\colon AA^{\prime}
of degree prime to
p
compatible with the
\mathbb{Q}
-homogeneous polarizations and the level structures.

By
[
20
, Proposition 7.1.5]
, the functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
is representable by a Deligne-Mumford stack which we denote by the same symbol
{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
. It is representable by a projective scheme over
\Spec O_{E_{r},(\nu)}
if
\mathbf{K}_{G}^{p}
is small enough.

7.2.
Semi-local formal moduli space of KRZ CM-triples

We fix a point
({\bm{A}},{\bm{\iota}},\bar{\bm{\lambda}},\bar{\bm{\eta}}^{p})

of
\mathcal{A}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(\bar{\kappa}_{{r,\nu}})
.
We denote by
\mathbb{X}
the
p
-divisible group of
{\bm{A}}
. The action
\bm{\iota}

induces an action
\iota_{\mathbb{X}}
of
O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
on
\mathbb{X}
and
\bm{\lambda}

induces a polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{X}
. This construction gives a CM-triple
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
of type
(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r)
over
\bar{\kappa}_{{r,\nu}}
. We introduce a semi-local version of the space
\mathcal{M}_{r}
in Definition
5.1.3
and
5.2.2
.

Definition 7.2.1
.

We define for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the functor
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
on the category
\Nilp_{O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}}
. Let
S
be a scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}}
. A point of
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)(S)
is given by the following data:

(1)

A CM-triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
of type

(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},r)
over
S

which satisfies the conditions
({\rm KC}_{r})
and
({\rm EC}_{r})
and is
(V,\varsigma)
-principally polarized;

(2)

An
O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear quasi-isogeny

\rho:\bar{X}:=X\times_{S}\bar{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{\kappa}_{r,{\nu}}}\bar{S}

such that, locally on
\bar{S}
,
\rho
respects the polarization
p^{i}\lambda
on
\bar{X}
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
up to a factor in
(O_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
.

The formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}
representing this functor is also denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)
.
We define the formal scheme

(7.2.1)

\mathcal{M}_{r}=\coprod\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{r}(i).

We define the algebraic group
J
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
such that

(7.2.2)

J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{\alpha\in\Aut^{\circ}_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{X})\;|\;\alpha^{\ast}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=\mu(\alpha)\lambda_{\mathbb{X}},\;\mu(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\}.

This group acts naturally on
\mathcal{M}_{r}
via the framing datum
\rho
.

We consider the decomposition
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=\prod_{v\mid p}(\mathbb{X}_{v},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{v}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})
and we set

(7.2.3)

J_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{\alpha_{v}\in\Aut^{\circ}_{K_{v}}(\mathbb{X}_{v})\;|\;\alpha_{v}^{\ast}(\lambda_{v})=\mu(\alpha_{v})\lambda_{v},\;\mu(\alpha_{v})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\}.

Following
[
20
, §7.2]
, we recall an explicit description of these groups. For this, it is
convenient to replace the bilinear form
\varsigma_{v}
by the

F_{v}
-bilinear form

\tilde{\varsigma}_{v}:V_{v}\times V_{v}\rightarrow F_{v},

which is defined by

\mathrm{T}(a\tilde{\varsigma}_{v}(x_{1},x_{2}))=\varsigma_{v}(ax_{1},x_{2}),\quad a\in F_{v},

for
\mathrm{T}(a)=\Trace_{F_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\vartheta^{-1}a
, where as usual
\vartheta\in O_{F}
is the different of
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Then
J_{v}
is explicitly given as follows:

(1)

For the special prime
v_{0}
, we obtain from the local CM triple
(\mathbb{X}_{v_{0}},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{v_{0}}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{v_{0}}})
a two-dimensional
K_{v_{0}}
-vector space
\bar{V}_{v_{0}}
with an anti-hermitian alternating form

\bar{\varsigma}_{v_{0}}:\bar{V}_{v_{0}}\times\bar{V}_{v_{0}}\rightarrow F_{v_{0}}.

This comes from the construction in
[
18
, §3, p. 1209]
when
K/F
is unramified, resp. the construction in
[
18
, §4, p. 1215]
in the ramified case (in loc. cit, the hermitian space is denoted by
C
and its anti-hermitian form is denoted by
h
). In either case
{\mathrm{inv}}(\bar{V}_{v_{0}})=1
. The contraction functor
[
20
, §5.2, §5.3]
furnishes an isomorphism between
J_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and

G(\bar{V}_{v_{0}},\bar{\varsigma}_{v_{0}})(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):=\{g\in\Aut_{K_{v_{0}}}(\bar{V}_{v_{0}})\;|\;\bar{\varsigma}_{v_{0}}(gx,gy)=\mu_{v_{0}}(g)\bar{\varsigma}_{v_{0}}(x,y),\;\;\mu_{v_{0}}(g)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\},

cf.
[
20
, (7.2.6)]
.

(2)

For a banal prime
v
, we consider the image
(C_{\mathbb{X}_{v}},\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})
by the polarized contraction functor

\mathfrak{C}_{r_{v},k}^{\rm pol}
of
(\mathbb{X}_{v},\iota_{\mathbb{X}_{v}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})

[
20
, Theorem 4.5.11]
. There is an isomorphism

(C_{\mathbb{X}_{v}},\phi_{\mathbb{X}_{v}})\cong(\Lambda_{v},\tilde{\varsigma}_{v})

and we obtain an isomorphism between
J_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and

G(V_{v},\tilde{\varsigma}_{v})(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):=\{g\in\Aut_{K_{v}}(\bar{V}_{v})\;|\;\tilde{\varsigma}_{v}(gx,gy)=\mu_{v}(g)\tilde{\varsigma}_{v}(x,y),\;\;\mu_{v}(g)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\},

cf.
[
20
, (7.2.8)]
.

These isomorphisms are induced by isomorphisms of algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. To use a uniform notation, we set
(\bar{V}_{v},\bar{\varsigma}_{v})=(V_{v},\tilde{\varsigma}_{v})
for
v\neq v_{0}
and set

\bar{G}_{v}=G(\bar{V}_{v},\bar{\varsigma}_{v})
for all
v\mid p
, in particular,
G_{v}=\bar{G}_{v}
for
v\neq v_{0}
. We thus have fixed an isomorphism
J_{v}\cong\bar{G}_{v}

for all
v\mid p
.

We define

\bar{V}_{p}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{v\mid p}\bar{V}_{v},

which is an
K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-module. Let

\bar{\varsigma}_{p}:\bar{V}_{p}\times\bar{V}_{p}\rightarrow F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}

be the orthogonal sum of the forms
\bar{\varsigma}_{v}
.

We define the algebraic group
\bar{G}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by

\bar{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):=G(\bar{V}_{p},\bar{\varsigma}_{p})=\{g\in\Aut_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\bar{V}_{p})\;|\;\bar{\varsigma}_{p}(gx,gy)=\mu(g)\bar{\varsigma}_{p}(x,y),\;\;\mu(g)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\}.

The previous discussions imply that we have
\bar{G}\simeq J
as algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
and

\bar{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\bar{G}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

is the subgroup of elements whose components have the same similitude factor.

In order to discuss the Weil descent datum, we define the group
\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by

\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{g\in\Aut_{K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\bar{V}_{p})\;|\;\bar{\varsigma}_{p}(gx,gy)=\mu(g)\bar{\varsigma}_{p}(x,y),\;\;\mu(g)\in p^{\mathbb{Z}}(O_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\}.

Note that this is a misuse of notation since
\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is not the set of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-points of an algebraic group. Then

\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p}\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

where we define
\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by

\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{g\in\Aut_{K_{v}}(\bar{V}_{v})\;|\;\bar{\varsigma}_{v}(gx,gy)=\mu_{v}(g)\bar{\varsigma}_{v}(x,y),\;\text{for}\;\mu_{v}(g)\in p^{\mathbb{Z}}O_{F_{v}}^{\times}\}.

Note that
\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\supset\bar{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

7.3.
The KRZ descent datum revisited

Let
\tau_{E_{r,\nu}}\in\Gal(\breve{E}_{r,\nu}/E_{r,\nu})
be the Frobenius
automorphism.
The relative Frobenius morphism

F_{\mathbb{X},\tau_{E_{r,\nu}}}:\mathbb{X}\rightarrow(\tau_{E_{r,\nu}})_{\ast}\mathbb{X}

defines a Weil descent datum on the functors,

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}:\mathcal{M}_{r}(i)(S)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}(i+f_{E_{r,\nu}})(S_{[\tau_{E_{r,\nu}}]}).

The goal of this section is to give a more explicit description of the descent datum of
[
20
, Corollary 7.2.7]
.

We define the group
\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as the union of the following sets
\hat{G}^{\prime}(i)
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\hat{G}^{\prime}(i)=\{(c,g_{v})\in p^{i}O_{F_{v_{0}}}^{\times}\times\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;|\;\mu_{v}(g_{v})\in p^{i}O_{F_{v}}^{\times}\;\text{for all}\;v\}.

The similitude map
\mu_{v_{0}}:\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow p^{\mathbb{Z}}O_{F_{v_{0}}}^{\times}

induces homomorphisms

\bar{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\quad\text{ and }\quad G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

We define
\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
to be the subgroup of elements

(c,g_{v})
such that
c\in O_{F_{v_{0}}}^{\times}
and

g_{v}\Lambda_{v}=\Lambda_{v}
.

For the next definition, we recall from §
3.1
the definition of
\Pi_{v}
(when
K_{v}/F_{v}
is ramified) and of
\pi_{v}
(when
K_{v}/F_{v}
is unramified or split).

Definition 7.3.1
.

We consider the following element

{w}^{\prime}_{r}=(w^{\prime}_{r,v_{0}},{w}^{\prime}_{r,v})\in\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
where

(1)

w^{\prime}_{r,v_{0}}=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}
.

(2)

If
K_{v}/F_{v}
is ramified,
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
is the multiplication

\Pi_{v}^{e_{v}f_{E_{r,\nu}}}:\bar{V}_{v}\longrightarrow\bar{V}_{v}.

(3)

If
K_{v}/F_{v}
is unramified, we define
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
as the multiplication

\pi_{v}^{e_{v}f_{E_{r,\nu}}/2}:\bar{V}_{v}\longrightarrow\bar{V}_{v}.

(4)

If
K_{v}=F_{v}\times F_{v}
is split, we have the decomposition

\bar{V}_{v}=\bar{V}_{v,1}\oplus\bar{V}_{v,2}
.
We consider the numbers
a_{1,v}
and
a_{2,v}
defined by
\dim\mathbb{X}_{v,1}=2a_{1,v}
and
\dim\mathbb{X}_{v,2}=2a_{2,v}
. We set

a_{1,E_{r,\nu}}=a_{1,v}\frac{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}{f_{v}},\quad\text{ resp. }\quad a_{2,E_{r,\nu}}=a_{2,v}\frac{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}{f_{v}}.

Then we have
a_{1,E_{r,\nu}}+a_{2,E_{r,\nu}}=e_{v}f_{E_{r,\nu}}
. We define
w^{\prime}_{v}
to be the multiplication by
\pi_{v}^{a_{1,E_{r,\nu}}}
on
\bar{V}_{v,1}
and the
multiplication by
\pi_{v}^{a_{2,E_{r,\nu}}}
on
\bar{V}_{v,1}
.

For a banal prime
v
, the element
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
is clearly an element of the center of
\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is considered as a subgroup of
K^{\times}_{v}
. The similitudes of these elements are given by

\mu_{v}(w^{\prime}_{r,v})=\pi^{e_{v}f_{E_{r,\nu}}}_{v}.

Therefore
\mu_{v}(w^{\prime}_{r,v})=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}u_{v}
for some
u_{v}\in O^{\times}_{F_{v}}
.

Let
v\neq v_{0}
. We consider the local CM type
\Phi^{+}_{v}=\Phi^{+}_{v,r}=\{\varphi\in\Phi_{v}\mid r_{\varphi}=2\}
.
Let

\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{v},E_{r,\nu}}:\mathrm{Res}_{E_{r,\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}

be the local reflex norm with respect to the data
(\Phi^{+}_{v},E_{r,\nu})
. We introduce the special element with respect to
(\Phi^{+}_{v},E_{r,\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{r,\nu}
in
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K^{\times}_{v}
,

w_{r,v}:=\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+}_{v},E_{r,\nu}}(\varpi_{r,\nu}),

cf. Construction
4.2.1
. Note that
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is contained in the center of
\bar{G}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Lemma 7.3.2
.

Let
v\neq v_{0}
, then the element
w_{r,v}
agrees with
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
up to a unit in
O_{K_{v}}
.

Proof.

We first remark that both
w_{r,v}
and
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
are elements of the center of
\bar{G}^{\prime}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is a subgroup of
K^{\times}_{v}
, therefore the statement of the lemma makes sense. But we have that
\mu_{v}(w^{\prime}_{r,v})=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}u_{v}
for some
u_{v}\in O^{\times}_{F_{v}}
. On the other hand,

(7.3.1)

\mu_{v}(w_{r,v})=\Nm_{E_{r,\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\varpi_{r,\nu})=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}u,\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}.

The lemma follows.
∎

We introduce the group

\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{(c,g_{v})\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\times\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,\;v\neq v_{0}}G_{v}\;|\;\mu_{v}(g_{v})=c,\;\forall v\neq v_{0}\}.

There are natural homomorphisms

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\quad\text{ and }\quad\bar{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

For the second map, we used that in the definition of
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
we can replace
G_{v}
by

\bar{G}_{v}
. In particular
the groups
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and

J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
act on
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
We denote by

\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
the subgroup of all

(c,g_{v})
such that
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and

g_{v}\Lambda_{v}=\Lambda_{v}
.

Construction 7.3.3
.

We have
w^{\prime}_{r,v_{0}}=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}
(cf. Definition
7.3.1
) and, by (
7.3.1
),
\mu_{v}(w_{r,v})=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}u
for
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
independent of
v\neq v_{0}
. Hence we can define the element

(7.3.2)

w_{r}=(uw^{\prime}_{r,v_{0}},w_{r,v})\in\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

which we refer to as the
special element of
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{r,\nu}
of
E_{r,\nu}
.

By
[
20
, Corollaries 6.3.4, 6.4.5, 6.5.3]
, the inclusion
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
induces a bijection

(7.3.3)

\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

By Lemma
7.3.2
, the class of the special element
w_{r}
maps under the bijection (
7.3.3
) to the class of
w^{\prime}_{r}
.

Proposition 7.3.4
.

There exists an isomorphism

{\mathcal{M}}_{r}(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

which is equivariant with respect to the natural action of
\bar{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

on both sides.

The Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{r}}
on the left-hand side corresponds on the right hand side to

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,E_{r,\nu}}(\xi),w_{r}g),\quad g\in\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

where
w_{r}
is the special element in
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{r,\nu}
.

Proof.

By
[
20
, Proposition 7.2.5]
, there is an isomorphism

(7.3.4)

{\mathcal{M}}_{r}(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

which is equivariant with respect to the action of
\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

on both sides. By loc. cit., the Weil descent datum
\omega_{{\mathcal{M}}_{r}}
relative to

O_{E_{r,\nu}}
on the left-hand side corresponds on the
right-hand side to

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{r,\nu}}}(\xi),w^{\prime}_{r}g),\quad g\in\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

We may multiply each
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
by a unit in
K^{\times}_{v}
in the
v
-component of
w^{\prime}_{r}
, cf. Definition
7.3.1
. By
[
20
, Remark 7.2.6]
, this does not change the assertion on the descent datum on both sides of (
7.3.4
). By Lemma
7.3.3
, we may replace
w^{\prime}_{r}
by
w_{r}
and the proposition follows.
∎

Now we can modify the main
p
-adic uniformization theorem
[
20
, Theorem 7.3.3]
in the following way. Recall
({\bm{A}},{\bm{\iota}},\bar{\bm{\lambda}},\bar{\bm{\eta}}^{p})\in\mathcal{A}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}(\bar{\kappa}_{{r,\nu}})
. We define

(7.3.5)

J(\mathbb{Q})=\{\alpha\in\Aut^{\circ}_{K}({\bm{A}})\;|\;\alpha^{\ast}({\bm{\lambda}})=\mu(\alpha){\bm{\lambda}},\;\mu(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\}.

Then
J(\mathbb{Q})
is the set of
\mathbb{Q}
-rational points of an algebraic group
J
over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
J(\mathbb{R})
is compact modulo center and the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-points can be identified with
J(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
from (
7.2.2
), cf.
[
20
, proof of Proposition 7.1.11]
. Moreover
J(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
as in
[
20
, (7.3.6)]
which defines an isomorphism
J(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})\simeq G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
.

Theorem 7.3.5
.

There is an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}
,

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{r,\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}\simeq J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}].

For varying
\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}
, the isomorphism is compatible with the action of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
through Hecke correspondences on both sides.

This isomorphism is compatible with the Weil descent data if we equip the right-hand side with the Weil descent datum

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{r,\nu}}}(\xi),w_{r}h,g)\quad h\in\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad g\in{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})

where
w_{r}
is the special element (
7.3.2
) in
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, for the uniformizer
\varpi_{r,\nu}
.

Proof.

By
[
20
, Theorem 7.3.3]
, we have the isomorphism

(7.3.6)

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}\simeq J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}].

whose descent datum is given by

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{r,\nu}}}(\xi),w^{\prime}_{r}h,g).

We can modify the
v
-component
w^{\prime}_{r,v}
of
w^{\prime}_{r}
by a unit in
O_{K_{v}}
for each banal prime
v
, cf.
[
20
, Remark 7.2.6]
; and we can modify
w^{\prime}_{r,v_{0}}
by a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. By Proposition
7.3.4
, we can therefore rewrite the descent datum as

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{r,\nu}}}(\xi),w_{r}h,g).

Now we have an isomorphism

\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

and the class of
w_{r}
in
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is sent to the class of
w^{\prime}_{r}
in
\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The assertion follows.
∎

Using the isomorphism
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\simeq\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as explained in
[
20
, (7.3.10)]
, we have the following isomorphisms

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{G}].

In the resulting isomorphism

(7.3.7)

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{r,\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}\simeq J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{G}]

the element
w_{r}
induces a non-explicit automorphism of
{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(its
v_{0}
-component is not well-determined) and thus the descent datum of the isomorphism
is non-explicit in this formulation.

Choose an extension
\varphi_{0}:K\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
of
\varphi_{0}:F\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
. We define a classical CM-type by

(7.3.8)

\Phi^{+}=\Phi^{+}_{\varphi_{0},r}=\{\varphi_{0}\}\cup\{\varphi\in\Phi\mid r_{\varphi}=2\}.

Let
E_{\Phi^{+}}
be the reflex field of
\Phi^{+}
. We define
E=E_{\Phi^{+}}\varphi_{0}(K)=E_{r}\varphi_{0}(K)
.

We next explain that if we extend the isomorphism in (
7.3.7
) to
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
, then the descent datum down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
becomes explicit. For this, we recall the semi-local special element
\widetilde{w}=\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+},E_{\nu}}(\varpi_{\nu})\in Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, relative to
(\Phi^{+},E_{\nu})
for the uniformizer
\varpi_{\nu}
of
E_{\nu}
, cf. Construction
6.1.1
. For the next statement, we note that
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
lies in the center of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Theorem 7.3.6
.

There is an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spf O_{{E}_{r,\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\simeq J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}_{G,p}\times G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}]

\displaystyle=J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{G}].

For varying
\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}
, this isomorphism is equivariant for the action of
G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
through Hecke correspondences on both sides.

This isomorphism is compatible with the Weil descent data down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
if we equip the right-hand side with the Weil descent datum

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\tilde{w}h,g),\quad h\in{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\quad g\in{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f}).

Proof.

We extend the isomorphism of Theorem
7.3.5
to
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
and obtain

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}})\times\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\hat{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}].

This isomorphism is compatible with the Weil descent data if we equip the right-hand side with the Weil descent datum

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),w_{r}^{f_{E_{\nu}}/f_{E_{r,\nu}}}h,g)

where
w_{r}
is the special element given by (
7.3.2
). Note that, regarding
\hat{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as a subset of
\prod_{v\mid p}\hat{G}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the
v_{0}
-component of
w_{r}
is given by
up^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}
, where
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
is given by
\Nm_{K_{v}/F_{v}}({w}_{v})=p^{f_{E_{r,\nu}}}u
, for any banal
v
. By Remark
4.2.2
,
\widetilde{w}_{v}
differs from
w_{r,v}^{f_{E_{\nu}}/f_{E_{r,\nu}}}
by a unit, for any banal
v
. Also
{\rm Nm}_{K_{v_{0}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\widetilde{w}_{v_{0}})=p^{f_{E_{\nu}}}
, up to a unit in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. It follows that the above Weil descent is given by

(\xi,h,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E_{\nu}}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}h,g),

as claimed.

∎

Remark 7.3.7
.

The above theorem is compatible with Theorem
6.2.1
in the case of the CM-type
\Phi^{+}=\Phi^{+}_{\varphi_{0},r}
used above. More precisely, there is a commutative diagram compatible with all the Weil descent data down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spf O_{{E}_{r,\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}_{G}\big].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here the left-vertical map is the natural map
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{r,\mathbf{K}_{G}}
induced by
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{p})(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{p})
and the right vertical map is induced by the natural map from
\widetilde{G}
to
G
, resp., from
\widetilde{J}
to
J
.

8.
KRZ Shimura curves vs RSZ Shimura curves

Now we compare the Shimura curves in
[
20
]
that are associated to the group
G
of unitary similitudes with the Shimura curves associated to
\widetilde{G}
.
Recall the exact sequence of algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
,

1\mathrm{U}G\overset{\mu}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{G}_{m}1.

To make the connection between the moduli stacks
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{G}}
, we make the assumption

(8.0.1)

\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathbf{K}_{G}\cap\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\quad\mu(\mathbf{K}_{G})=\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}.

Then
\mathbf{K}_{G}
and
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
determine each other uniquely. Indeed,
\mathbf{K}_{G}
is the maximal open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
whose intersection with
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is equal to
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
.

We now pass to deeper level structures at places over
p
. For each place
v\mid p

of
F
, we consider first the open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{G,v}\subset G_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is defined as the quasi-parahoric given by
\mathrm{Stab}_{G_{v}}(\Lambda_{v})
.
We will assume that there exist places
v
which are banal
since our deeper level structures exist only in this case.

For the special prime
v_{0}
we set

\mathbf{K}_{G,v_{0}}^{\star}=\mathbf{K}_{G,v_{0}}
. For each banal
v
, we choose an open subgroup of

\mathbf{K}_{G,v}^{\star}\subset\mathbf{K}_{G,v}
.
We set

\mathbf{K}_{G,p}^{\star}=\{g=(g_{v})\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;|\;g_{v}\in\mathbf{K}_{G,v}^{\star},\forall v\mid p\}.

This says that
\mu_{v}(g_{v})
is independent of

v
. We also introduce

(8.0.2)

\mathbf{K}_{G,p}^{\star,{v_{0}}}=\{(g_{v})\in\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G,v}\;|\;\mu_{v}(g_{v})=c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\;\text{independent of}\;v\}.

This is a subgroup of

G^{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{(g_{v})\in\prod\nolimits_{v\mid p,v\neq v_{0}}G_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;|\;\mu_{v}(g_{v})=c\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times},\;\text{independent of}\;v\}.

8.1.
At the level of moduli problems over
E_{r}

Let
r
be a special CM type of rank
2
with reflex field
E_{r}
and let
\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}
be a classical CM type with reflex field
E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}
. Let
E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}^{\mathrm{ab}}
be the maximal abelian extension of
E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}
, and let
E^{\sharp}=E_{r}E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}^{\mathrm{ab}}
, an abelian extension of
E=E_{r}E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}^{\star}_{G}={\mathbf{K}}^{\star}_{G,p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{G}
. Consider the following moduli problem
\mathcal{A}_{r,{\mathbf{K}}^{\star}_{G}}
on
({\rm LNSch}/E_{r})
. It associates to an
E_{r}
-scheme
S
the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta})
where all data are the same as in Definition
7.1.1
, except that (3) is replaced by

(
3^{\prime}
) A
\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}
-class
\bar{\eta}
of
K
-linear similitudes

\eta\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}(A)V\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}.

Here the rational Tate module is equipped with its natural anti-hermitian form arising by contraction from its polarization form.

When
\mathbf{K}_{G}^{p}
is sufficiently small, this moduli problem is represented by a projective scheme
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G},E}
which is the
canonical model
of the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}(G,X_{G})
over
E
. Let
A^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}
be the coarse moduli space associated to the Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
. Therefore
A^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}
parametrizes the isomorphism classes of objects of
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
.

Proposition 8.1.1
.

There is an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}\times_{\Spec E}\Spec E^{\sharp}\simeq\coprod_{A_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\bar{}\mathbb{Q})}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G},E_{r}}\times_{\Spec E_{r}}\Spec E^{\sharp}.

In other words, over
E^{\sharp}
, the scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
becomes a disjoint sum of copies of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G},E_{r}}
.

Proof.

Let
({A_{0}},{\iota_{0}},{\lambda_{0}})\in\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(O_{E^{\sharp}})
. We fix an isomorphism
\widehat{}\mathrm{T}(A_{0,E^{\sharp}})=\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{O_{K}}\widehat{O}_{K}
. Since all elements of
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
are isogenous, all rational Tate modules of elements of
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
are identified with
V_{0}\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}
. Therefore we obtain a morphism

(8.1.1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}\times_{\Spec E}\Spec E^{\sharp}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G},E_{r}}\times_{\Spec E_{r}}\Spec E^{\sharp}.

It sends a tuple
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{p})
to
(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{\prime p})
, where
\bar{\eta}^{\prime p}\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A)V\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}^{p}_{K,f}
is induced from

\eta^{p}\colon\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A_{0},A)=\Hom_{K}(\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A_{0}),\widehat{V}^{p}(A))\widetilde{V}\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p}=\Hom_{K}(V_{0},V)\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p},

after identifying the source of the map with
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}(A)
and the target with
V\otimes_{K}\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{p}
. Indeed, by hypothesis (
8.0.1
), the
\mathbf{K}_{G}^{\star,p}
-class of
\eta^{\prime p}
is well-determined by the
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}^{p}
-class of
\eta^{p}
. Furthermore, by the theory of complex multiplication, the Galois action of
\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}/E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}})
on
A_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\bar{}\mathbb{Q})
factors through its maximal abelian quotient, hence the action of
\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}/E^{\sharp})
on
\widehat{}\mathrm{V}^{p}({A}_{0,E^{\sharp}})
is trivial. Hence the Galois invariance of
\bar{\eta}^{\prime p}
follows from the Galois invariance of
\bar{\eta}^{p}
. Finally, the sign condition (iii) from Definition
7.1.1
for
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta}^{\prime p})
follows from the identities

{\mathrm{inv}}^{r}_{v}(A_{0,\bar{s}},\iota_{0,\bar{s}},\lambda_{0,\bar{s}},A_{\bar{s}},\iota_{\bar{s}},\lambda_{\bar{s}})={\mathrm{inv}}_{v}^{r}(A_{\bar{s}},\iota_{\bar{s}},\lambda_{\bar{s}}),\quad\quad{\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(\widetilde{V})={\mathrm{inv}}_{v}(V).

Indeed, for instance, the last identity follows from the identity

\wedge^{2}(V_{0}\otimes V)=V_{0}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\wedge^{2}(V).

Since this construction is clearly functorial, we obtain the morphism (
8.1.1
).
Letting
({A_{0}},{\iota_{0}},{\lambda_{0}})
vary through
A_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\bar{}\mathbb{Q})
, we obtain the desired decomposition in the proposition.
∎

8.2.
At the level of moduli problems over
O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}

Recall from
[
20
, Definition 7.4.5]
the scheme
\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}
over
\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}
. To define it, we first define a functor
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}
on the
category of schemes
S
over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}
.
A point of
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}(S)
consists of
the following data:

(1)

A point
(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta}^{p})
of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{G}}(S)
;

(2)

A class
\overline{\eta}^{v_{0}}_{p}
of isomorphisms of
p
-adic étale sheaves

\eta^{v_{0}}_{p}\colon\underline{\Hom}_{O_{K}}(\mathbb{X}_{0}^{v_{0}},A[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}})\Lambda^{v_{0}}:=\prod\nolimits_{v\neq v_{0},v\mid p}\Lambda_{v}\mod\mathbf{K}_{G,p}^{\star,{v_{0}}},

which respect forms on both sides up to a constant in
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
, cf.
[
20
, just before Proposition 7.4.10]
.

The morphism
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{G}}
forgetting the datum
(2)
(or its alternative version)
is a finite étale covering of formal schemes. Since we assume that

\mathbf{K}^{p}_{G}
is small enough,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{G}}
is a proper scheme
over
\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}
. By the algebraization theorem, there is a unique
finite étale morphism of schemes over
\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}

(8.2.1)

\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{G}}\times_{\Spec\,O_{E_{r,\nu}}}\Spec\,O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}},

such that the
p
-adic completion of
\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}
is

\hat{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}
.

Proposition 8.2.1
.

There is an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}\times_{\Spec O_{E}}\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq\coprod_{A_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}(\bar{\kappa}_{\nu})}{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}\times_{\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{r,\nu}}}\Spec O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}.

In other words, over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
, the scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
becomes a disjoint sum of copies of
\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{\mathbf{K}^{\star}_{G}}
.

Proof.

Note that the index sets in Propositions
8.1.1
and
8.2.1
can be identified, as follows from the fact that
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}
is finite and étale over
\Spec O_{E_{\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}}}
. Since both sides are proper over the base, we may pass to the formal completions on both sides. Note that
\mathbb{X}_{0}^{v_{0}}
can be identified with
A_{0}[p^{\infty}]^{v_{0}}
, cf. (
2.3.3
). Hence
\eta_{p}^{v_{0}}
defines a CL-level structure, and this extends the isomorphism of Proposition
8.1.1
to
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
.
∎

Part IV
Shimura curves for the unitary group

9.
The integral LSV for the unitary group

Let
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension. Let
K/F
be a quadratic extension, and let
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}
be a
K/F
-unitary group of size
2
over
F
which is anisotropic. Let
\mathrm{U}=\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{U}^{\prime})
. We also fix an embedding
\varphi_{0}:K\rightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

9.1.
Formulation of the result

Let
\Phi^{+}
be a local CM-type for
K/F
containing
\varphi_{0}
. We define a conjugacy class of cocharacters
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{U}_{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
from
\Phi^{+}
, as follows. Namely, we characterize
\mu
by the composite

(9.1.1)

\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathrm{U}_{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\simeq\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi^{+}}\mathrm{GL}_{2,{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}}.

We demand that
\{\mu\}
has component
(1,0)
at
\varphi_{0}
and
(0,0)
at all other components (as conjugacy classes of cocharacters of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
). The reflex field
E(\mu)
of
\mu
is the subfield of
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
fixed by

\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/E(\mu)):=\{\tau\in\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mid\tau\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{0}\}.

It follows that
E(\mu)
is equal to
\varphi_{0}(K)=K
.

Let
b\in\mathrm{U}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
represent the unique element of
{B}(\mathrm{U},\mu^{-1})
, which is basic. Let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is a maximal quasi-parahoric subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. When
K/F
is unramified, then
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}
is a parahoric; if
K/F
is ramified, then
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}
contains a parahoric with index
2
. By
[
22
]
we can associate to the local Shimura datum
(\mathrm{U},b,\mu,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}})
, the integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}
. This is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}(\mu)}
with natural descent datum to
\Spf O_{E(\mu)}=\Spf O_{K}
.

We introduce the following finite extension of
K
contained in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,

(9.1.2)

E(\varphi_{0})=\bigcap\nolimits_{\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{\prime,+}}E_{\Phi^{\prime,+}}K,

(intersection of the join with
K
of the reflex fields of all CM-types
\Phi^{\prime,+}
of
K
containing
\varphi_{0}
). We remark that when
F=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, then
E(\varphi_{0})
agrees with
K
.

The following is the main result of this section. The proof will be given in the next subsection.

Theorem 9.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}
be the integral local Shimura variety associated to the datum
(\mathrm{U},b,\mu,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}})
defined above. Then the Weil descent datum on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}
is effective and defines a formal scheme
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{K}}
over
\Spf O_{K}
. There is an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})}
,

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{K}}\times_{\Spf O_{K}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})}.

Remark 9.1.2
.

We recall from §
2.4
, lines below Theorem
2.4.5
, that we conjecture that this isomorphism is induced by an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{K}
.
When
F=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, then
E(\varphi_{0})=K
, and the above isomorphism is simply

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{K}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{K}.

In this case, the expectation is true.

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
9.1.1

To prove the main theorem stated in the last subsection, we introduce two more integral local Shimura varieties.

Let
\Phi_{0}^{+}
be a local CM-type with
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi_{0}^{+}
. We define the torus
\mathrm{T}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with

\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\{t\in K^{\times}:\Nm_{K/F}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\}.

We consider the cocharacter
\mu_{0}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
obtained from the local CM-type
\Phi_{0}^{+}
. Namely,
\mu_{0}
is defined so that the composite

\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}\overset{\mu_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{T}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\subset\prod\nolimits_{\varphi\in\Phi_{0}^{+}}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p})_{\varphi}

is given by the diagonal embedding. Let
E_{0}=E_{\Phi_{0}^{+}}
be the reflex field of
\Phi_{0}^{+}
. Let
b_{0}\in\mathrm{T}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
represent the unique element
[b_{0}]
of
{B}(\mathrm{T},\mu_{0}^{-1})
. Let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
be the maximal compact open subgroup of
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By
[
22
]
we can associate to the local Shimura datum
(\mathrm{T},b_{0},\mu_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}})
the integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
. This is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{{0}}}
with natural descent datum down to
\Spf O_{E_{{0}}}
.

Let
\widetilde{G}=\mathrm{U}\times\mathrm{T}
. We obtain the cocharacter

\widetilde{\mu}=\mu\times\mu_{0}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,,\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}.

Also, let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
. Then
\widetilde{b}=(b,b_{0})\in\widetilde{G}(\breve{}\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{U}(\breve{}\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathrm{T}(\breve{}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a representative of the unique basic element in
{B}(\widetilde{G},\widetilde{\mu}^{-1})
. By
[
22
]
, we can associate to the local Shimura datum
(\widetilde{G},\widetilde{b},\widetilde{\mu},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}})
the integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
, a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
with natural descent datum to
\Spf O_{E}
. Here
E=E_{0}K
is the reflex field for this local Shimura datum.

The integral local Shimura varieties
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
are of PEL-type and therefore they are isomorphic to the corresponding RZ spaces, cf.
[
27
, lecture 25]
2
2

2

Note that in loc. cit., the Weil descent datum is ignored.
. Let us explain this for
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
. We fix
(V,\varsigma)
as in (
5.0.1
) such that
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}=\mathrm{U}(V)
. Let
r=r_{\Phi^{+}_{0}}
be the generalized CM-type of rank
2
, special wrt
{\varphi_{0}}_{|F}
, defined by

r_{\varphi}=2,\quad\varphi\in\Phi^{+}_{0}\setminus\{\varphi_{0}\}.

Then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
introduced in Definition
5.1.1
for the given
\Phi^{+}_{0}
and
r
. Similarly,

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
as in Definition
4.2.3
. Note that
E=E_{0}K=E_{r}K
, where
E_{r}
is the reflex field of the local CM-type
r
.

Proof of Theorem
9.1.1
.

The functoriality of integral local Shimura varieties
[
22
, §2.4]
furnishes the following product decomposition

(9.2.1)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\simeq\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}},

with compatible Weil descent datum down to
O_{E}
on both sides. Here, to simplify the notation, we have indicated the base changes by the index
O_{\breve{E}}
(from
O_{\breve{K}}
to
O_{\breve{E}}
for the first factor, resp. from
O_{\breve{E}_{0}}
to
O_{\breve{E}}
for the second factor).

We have an explicit expression for the LHS of (
9.2.1
). Indeed, we have
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
. Hence by Proposition
5.1.6
, we have

(9.2.2)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}\simeq(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},

where we used that
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
.

We also have an explicit expression for the second factor on the RHS of (
9.2.1
). Indeed,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}\simeq{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
so that, by Proposition
4.2.5
, we have

(9.2.3)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}\simeq\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}.

Note that we have a commutative diagram of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
,

(9.2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here the vertical map on the left is the projection morphism from (
9.2.1
). The vertical map on the right comes from the moduli description of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
, cf. (
5.1.11
).
Comparing the individual factors of (
9.2.1
) with (
9.2.2
) and using the fact that

(9.2.5)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}\simeq{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}\simeq\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},

it follows from considering the vertical fibers of the commutative diagram (
9.2.4
) that we have an isomorphism

(9.2.6)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}.

By the equivariance of the vertical projections for the action of
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and the transitivity of the action on the target, this isomorphism is independent of the fiber.

Moreover, this isomorphism is compatible with Weil descent data down to
O_{E}
on both sides. To see this, we use the compatibility of the Weil descent data on the two sides of the isomorphism (
9.2.1
)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}.

It follows that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}\big)^{(\tau_{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}\big)^{(\tau_{E})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
\alpha_{1}
is given by
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})(\omega_{\mathrm{U},E}(\xi_{1}),\omega_{\mathrm{T},E}(\xi_{2}))
, with
\omega_{\mathrm{U},E}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{T},E}
the natural Weil descent data for
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{\breve{E}}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu_{0},b_{0},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}},O_{\breve{E}}}
down to
O_{E}
, and where
\alpha_{2}
is given by
(\xi,t)(\omega_{\Omega,E}(\xi),\widetilde{w}t)
, with
\omega_{\Omega,E}
the natural Weil descent datum for
\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}}
and with
\widetilde{w}
as in the statement of Proposition
5.1.6
. Now the Weil descent datum
\omega_{\mathrm{T},E}
is given in terms of the identification (
9.2.3
) by
t{w_{r_{0}}}^{f_{E}/f_{E_{0}}}t
, where
w_{r_{0}}
is the special element from Proposition
4.2.5
. But
\widetilde{w}
and
{w_{r_{0}}}^{f_{E}/f_{E_{0}}}
differ by a unit (both have
K/F
-norm
p^{f_{E}}
, up to a unit), hence have the same residue class modulo
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Since the isomorphism (
9.2.6
) is independent of the fiber, it follows that (
9.2.6
) is compatible with the Weil descent data, as claimed.

It follows that the descent datum on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E}}
down to
O_{E}
is effective, defining the descended formal scheme
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E}}
over
\Spf O_{E}
, and that the isomorphism (
9.2.6
) is induced by an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{E}

(9.2.7)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{E}.

Now we let
\Phi^{+}_{0}
vary. We claim that we can descend the isomorphism (
9.2.7
) to the smaller field
E(\varphi_{0})
. To prove this, it suffices to consider two such local CM-types, say
\Phi^{+}_{0}
and
\Phi^{+,\prime}_{0}
. Then we obtain two local reflex fields
E=E_{0}K
and
E^{\prime}=E_{0}^{\prime}K
. Let
L
be the composite of
E
and
E^{\prime}
. To establish our claim, it suffices to show that the isomorphisms from (
9.2.7
)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{E},\quad\text{resp.}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E^{\prime}}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{E^{\prime}},

give rise via base-change to
O_{L}
to the same isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{L}}\simeq\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{L}.

By our construction, this boils down to showing that the Weil descent datum on both sides of

(9.2.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{\breve{E}}}\simeq(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}})\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}}}(\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}})_{O_{\breve{E}}},

\displaystyle\quad\text{resp.}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{\breve{E}^{\prime}}}\simeq(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}^{\prime}})\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{E}^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}})_{O_{\breve{E}^{\prime}}}

induce the same Weil descent datum on both sides of

(9.2.9)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{b},\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{\breve{L}}}\simeq(\hat{\Omega}_{F}\times_{\Spf O_{F}}\Spf O_{\breve{L}})\times_{\Spf O_{\breve{L}}}(\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}})_{O_{\breve{L}}}.

On the left-hand side, this is clear. By Proposition
5.1.6
, the Weil descent datum down to
O_{E}
in the first line on the right-hand side of (
9.2.8
) is given by

(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{E}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}_{E}g),\quad g\in\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Here we recall
\widetilde{w}_{E}={\mathfrak{r}}_{\Phi_{0}^{+},E}(\varpi_{E})
is the special element relative to
(\Phi_{0}^{+},E)
for a uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
of
E
as in Construction
4.2.1
. A similar formula applies to the second line of (
9.2.8
) and makes explicit the Weil descent datum down to
O_{{E}^{\prime}}
, where we use the special element
\widetilde{w}_{E^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{r}_{\Phi^{+,\prime}_{0},E^{\prime}}(\varpi_{E^{\prime}})
relative to
(\Phi_{0}^{+,\prime},E^{\prime})
for a uniformizer
\varpi_{E^{\prime}}
of
E
. Note that the Weil descent datum depends only on the image of
\widetilde{w}_{\ast}
in
\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{T}}
for
\ast\in\{E,E^{\prime}\}
, which means we can always modify the element
\widetilde{w}_{\ast}
by a unit in
O_{K}
.

Let
f_{L}
be the inertia degree of
L
. Then the descent datum induced on the RHS of (
9.2.9
) by the first line of (
9.2.8
) is given by
(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{L}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}_{E}^{f_{L}/f_{E}}g)
, where
f_{E}
denotes the inertia degree of
E
. Similarly, the descent datum induced on the RHS of (
9.2.9
) by the second line of (
9.2.8
) is given by
(\xi,g)(\omega_{\Omega,{L}}(\xi),\widetilde{w}_{E^{\prime}}^{f_{L}/f_{E^{\prime}}}g)
, where
f_{E^{\prime}}
denotes the inertia degree of
E^{\prime}
. It suffices to show that
\widetilde{w}_{E}^{f_{L}/f_{E}}
and
\widetilde{w}_{E^{\prime}}^{f_{L}/f_{E^{\prime}}}
differ by a unit. This follows from the identities

\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w}_{E})=p^{f_{E}}
and
\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w}_{E^{\prime}})=p^{f_{E^{\prime}}}
(up to units) which imply

\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w}^{f_{L}/f_{E}}_{E})=p^{f_{L}}=\Nm_{K/F}(\widetilde{w}^{f_{L}/f_{E^{\prime}}}_{E^{\prime}})

(up to units).

At this point, we have proved the /effectivity of the Weil descent datum on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}
down to the finite extension
O_{E(\varphi_{0})}
of
O_{K}
, which then induces a usual descent datum on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E(\varphi_{0})}}
for the finite extension
O_{E(\varphi_{0})}
of
O_{K}
. This descent datum is in fact effective. Indeed, this follows from the fact that the canonical bundle on the special fiber of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E(\varphi_{0})}}
is “ample” (i.e., the restriction to any finite type closed subscheme is ample). This latter fact is well-known for
\hat{\Omega}_{F}
and follows from the fact that the quotient of
\hat{\Omega}_{F}
by a sufficiently small discrete cocompact subgroup of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F)
is a stable curve over
O_{F}
in the sense of Deligne-Mumford, comp. the argument in the proof of
[
20
, Theorem 7.3.3]
. We obtain the formal scheme
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mu,b,\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{K}}
with the property in the statement of Theorem
9.1.1
.
∎

10.
p
-adic uniformization of Shimura curves for the unitary group

Now we consider the global case. Let
K/F
be a CM-field, with a fixed embedding
\varphi_{0}\colon K\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
. We denote by
w_{0}
the archimedean place of
F
induced from the embedding
F\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
. Let
p
be a prime number and fix an embedding
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We denote by
v_{0}
the
p
-adic place induced by
F\bar{}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We assume that
v_{0}
does not split in
K
. We will identify
F
and
K
with their images via
\varphi_{0}
in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
. .

Let
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}
be a
K/F
-unitary group of size
2
and set
\mathrm{U}=\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{U}^{\prime})
. We assume that
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}_{v_{0}}
is anisotropic. Consider the Shimura datum
{X}_{\mathrm{U}}
given by the conjugacy class of homomorphisms

(10.0.1)

h_{\mathrm{U}}\colon\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow\mathrm{U}_{\mathbb{R}}

that we define now.
Fix a CM type
\Phi^{+}
for
K/F
with
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{+}
. We choose for each
\varphi\in\Phi^{+}
a
\mathbb{C}
-basis of
V\otimes_{K,\varphi}\mathbb{C}
such that the anti-hermitian form is given by
\diag(i\cdot 1_{r_{\varphi}},(-i)\cdot 1_{r_{\bar{\varphi}}})
. Then we can write

\mathrm{U}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\bar{}\mathbb{Q}=\prod\nolimits_{\Phi^{+}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}/\bar{}\mathbb{Q}.

Correspondingly
h_{\mathrm{U}}=(h_{\varphi})_{\varphi\in\Phi^{+}}
. We set

(10.0.2)

h_{\varphi}(z)=\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \diag(1,z/\bar{z}),&\quad\varphi=\varphi_{0}\\
1,&\quad\varphi\neq\varphi_{0}.\\
\end{aligned}\end{cases}

Then the reflex field
E(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
of
(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
is given by the fixed field of

\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}/E(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}}))=\{\tau\in\Gal(\bar{}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Q})\mid\tau\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{0}\}.

Therefore
E(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})=\varphi_{0}(K)=K
.

For an open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, there is a Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
over
\Spec K
,
whose complex points are given by

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash[\Omega_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}].

Note this Shimura variety is of abelian type but not of Hodge type. We denote by
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})_{K_{v_{0}}}
the base change over
K_{v_{0}}
, and analogously for any extension of
K_{v_{0}}
.

The goal of this section is to establish
p
-adic uniformization for this Shimura curve over an extension of
K_{v_{0}}
.

10.1.
Construction of integral models

We take the open compact subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
of the form
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}
, where
{\mathbf{K}}^{p}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is sufficiently small. Let us write
\mathrm{U}_{p}=\mathrm{U}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathrm{U}_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\prod_{v\mid p}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where we denote by
\mathrm{U}_{v}={\rm Res}_{F_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{U}^{\prime}_{v})
the corresponding unitary group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then we further impose that

(10.1.1)

{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},p}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}\cdot{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p},

where
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}=\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and where
{\mathbf{K}}^{v_{0}}_{\mathrm{U},p}\subset\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is arbitrary. Note that this class of open compact subgroups is stable under conjugation by
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Note that
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
is a quasi-parahoric subgroup of
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(recall that
\mathrm{U}_{v_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is compact by assumption).

We first consider the case where

(10.1.2)

{\mathbf{K}}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},p}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}\cdot\prod_{v\neq v_{0}}{\mathbf{K}}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},v}.

Here for
v\neq v_{0}
we impose that
{\mathbf{K}}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},v}
is a maximal parahoric subgroup of
\mathrm{U}_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is contained in the stabilizer of an almost selfdual lattice
\Lambda_{v}
in
V_{v}
, cf. the Notation section in the Introduction. Then
{\mathbf{K}}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},p}
is a quasi-parahoric subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. It is even a parahoric subgroup if
v_{0}
is unramified in
K
since then
{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}
is a parahoric.

We will assume the existence of the
canonical integral model

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}^{\circ}}
of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
over
O_{K_{v_{0}}}
. This is a normal scheme proper and flat over
\Spec O_{K_{v_{0}}}
which admits a unique characterization, cf.
[
21
, Conjecture 4.2.2]
. When
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},p}
is a parahoric and
p\neq 2
, the canonical integral model exists, cf.
[
7
]
. When
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U},p}
is a quasi-parahoric or
p=2
, the existence of the canonical integral model seems still unknown. Compare also with Remark
10.1.2
below.

Proposition 10.1.1
.

There exists a unique extension
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})_{K_{v_{0}}}
as a normal flat
O_{K_{v_{0}}}
-scheme, for every
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
of the form (
10.1.1
), compatible with the transition morphisms for varying
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
, such that the following two conditions are satisfied.

(1)

For
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}=\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}
, as in (
10.1.2
), the model coincides with the canonical integral model.

(2)

The transition morphisms
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
for
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
are finite étale.

Proof.

The uniqueness is easy: for
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}
, we see that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
is the normalization of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}}
in
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})_{K_{v_{0}}}
. This defines
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
for all sufficiently small
{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
. If
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
is a normal subgroup, then the action of
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}/\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}
on
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})_{K_{v_{0}}}
extends to
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}}/(\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}/\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}})
. This extends the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
to all
{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
. The same argument also handles the existence. It remains to show that the transition morphisms are finite étale. For this, we introduce two more Shimura varieties which are of PEL type.

Recall the CM-type
\Phi^{+}
we used to define the Shimura datum
(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
. In analogy to the local case (where we constructed the pair
(\mathrm{T},\mu_{0})
), we obtain a Shimura datum
(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
. Let
\widetilde{G}=\mathrm{U}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
. We obtain the Shimura datum
(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
, where
h_{\widetilde{G}}=h_{\mathrm{U}}\times h_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
. The reflex field for
(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
is equal to
E=E_{\Phi^{+}}K
. For the corresponding Shimura varieties we have the following isomorphism over
\Spec E
,

(10.1.3)

{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})\simeq{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})_{E}\times_{\Spec E}{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})_{E},

where we define

(10.1.4)

\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}.

We can identify the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
with the PEL type Shimura variety of Definition
2.2.1
. To this end, let
(V,\varsigma)
as in (
2.1.1
) such that
\mathrm{U}^{\prime}
is equal the isometry group
\mathrm{U}(V,\varsigma)
. Let
r
be the CM-type of rank
2
, special with respect to
\varphi_{0}
, such that its canonical CM-type is given by
\Phi^{+}
. Recall that this means
r_{\varphi_{0}}=r_{\bar{\varphi}_{0}}=1
and
r_{\varphi}=2
for
\varphi\in\Phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{+}}\setminus\{\varphi_{0}\}
. Then
E=E_{r}K
. We obtain an isomorphism between
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})

with the Shimura variety
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
of Definition
2.2.1
.

The decomposition (
10.1.3
)
extends to canonical integral models over
O_{E_{\nu}}
,

(10.1.5)

\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{E_{\nu}}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}}\times_{\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}}\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{E_{\nu}}},

where
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Here we denoted by an index the base changes
O_{K}O_{E_{\nu}}
, resp.
O_{E_{\Phi^{+}}}O_{E_{\nu}}
, on the first, resp. second, factor on the RHS.

Remark 10.1.2
.

Let us clarify. We are assuming the existence of the canonical integral model for
