{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
over
O_{K_{v_{0}}}
. Since we are assuming that for
v\neq v_{0}
, the open compact
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v}^{\circ}
is a parahoric stabilizer of
\Lambda_{v}
, it follows that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{\circ}
is a parahoric iff
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},v_{0}}^{\circ}
is a parahoric, which is true iff
v_{0}
is unramified in
K
. The existence of the canonical integral model for
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathrm{U},X_{\mathrm{U}})
over
O_{K_{v_{0}}}
is known when
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U},p}^{\circ}
is a parahoric and
p\neq 2
, cf.
[
7
]
. Thus our result is conditional iff
v_{0}
is ramified in
K
or
p=2
.

On the other hand, the existence of the canonical integral models of the PEL-type Shimura varieties
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
over
O_{E_{\nu}}
and of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
over
O_{E_{\Phi^{+},\nu}}
follows from
[
8
, Theorem 4.2.3]
(there is no assumption on the quasi-parahoric
\mathbf{K}_{p}
in loc. cit., nor is it assumed that
p\neq 2
). Furthermore, the canonical integral model of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
is equal to the
p
-integral model
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G},O_{E_{\nu}}}}
of Definition
2.3.2
, and, similarly, the canonical integral model of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
is equal to the
p
-integral model
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{E_{\nu}}}
of §
2.2
. Indeed, this follows from the construction in
[
8
, Theorem 4.2.3]
.

Now let
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
be arbitrary, and let
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}
as in (
10.1.4
). We define integral models
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
of
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
, together with their transition maps through normalization, just like for
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
, starting with
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}}}
. Then these
p
-integral models coincide with those of Definition
2.3.2
(use the normality of those models). By the regularity of
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{E}}
, we obtain a product decomposition

(10.1.6)

\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{E_{\nu}}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}}\times_{\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}}\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{E_{\nu}}},

for any
\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}\times\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
. It follows from
[
20
, §7.4]
that the transition maps for
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}
are finite étale.

Let
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}}\subset\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}
. By the universal property of the normalization, we obtain a commutative diagram of
O_{E_{\nu}}
-schemes, in which the vertical maps are the transition morphisms, and the horizontal maps are the projection morphisms from the decomposition (
10.1.6
),

(10.1.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{E_{\nu}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{E_{\nu}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}}}

By the above, this diagram is cartesian. Since the left vertical map is finite étale, so is the right vertical map.
∎

10.2.
p
-adic uniformization of canonical integral models

In this section, we prove a uniformization theorem, in the following form.
Analogously to the local case, we introduce the finite extension of
K
contained in
\bar{}\mathbb{Q}
,

(10.2.1)

E(\varphi_{0})=\bigcap\nolimits_{\varphi_{0}\in\Phi^{\prime,+}}E_{\Phi^{\prime,+}}K.

Theorem 10.2.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}})^{\wedge}
be the formal completion of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}}
along its special fiber, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf O_{K_{v_{0}}}
. Then there exists an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}
,

(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{K_{v_{0}}}}{\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}}\simeq{J_{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})}\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{E(\varphi_{0})_{\nu}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}\big].

Here
J_{\mathrm{U}}
is an inner form of
\mathrm{U}
which is compact at all archimedean places and is quasi-split at
v_{0}
and is locally isomorphic to
\mathrm{U}
at all other places of
F
(note that by the Hasse principle for adjoint groups,
J_{\mathrm{U}}
is uniquely determined by these conditions). The action on the RHS is through an action on
\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}
via an identification of
J_{\mathrm{U},v_{0},{\mathrm{ad}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(F_{v_{0}})
and an action on
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}
.

Proof.

We proceed in analogy to the proof of Theorem
9.1.1
. In the last subsection, we used the fixed CM-type
\Phi^{+}
which was used to define the Shimura datum for
\mathrm{U}
. Now we vary this CM-type. Let
\Phi_{0}^{+}
be any CM-type with
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi_{0}^{+}
. As before, we define the torus
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
with

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})=\{t\in K^{\times}:\Nm_{K/F}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\}

and use
\Phi_{0}^{+}
to define a Shimura datum
(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z})
.
Let
E_{0}=E_{\Phi_{0}^{+}}
be the reflex field of
\Phi_{0}^{+}
. Let
\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
be the maximal compact open subgroup of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})
. We obtain the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},X_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
, with integral model
\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0}
over
O_{E_{0}}
.

Let
\widetilde{G}=\mathrm{U}\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}
, with its Shimura datum
X_{\widetilde{G}}=X_{\mathrm{U}}\times X_{Z}
. The corresponding reflex field is
E=E_{0}K
. As in the last subsection, we can identify the Shimura variety
{\rm Sh}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},X_{\widetilde{G}})
with
\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{G}},E}
.

The identification (
10.1.6
) (now for
\Phi_{0}^{+}
instead of
\Phi^{+}
) yields isomorphisms compatible with changes in
\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}}

(10.2.2)

\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}},O_{E_{\nu}}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}}\times_{\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}}\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{E_{\nu}}}.

We have an explicit expression for the completion of the LHS along its special fiber. Indeed, by Theorem
6.2.1
, we have

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq\widetilde{J}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\widetilde{G}}\big].

By Corollary
6.2.3
, we obtain an isomorphism of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
, with Weil descent data down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
,

(10.2.3)

(\widetilde{}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\widetilde{G}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq{J_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}\big]\times Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

We now want to compare the product decompositions of (
10.2.2
) and (
10.2.3
). We have an isomorphism

(10.2.4)

\mathcal{A}^{[\Lambda_{0}]}_{0,O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\simeq Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathbf{K}^{\circ}_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}},

and the second projections in (
10.2.2
) and (
10.2.3
) are compatible with this identification. It
follows from considering the fibers that we have an isomorphism

(10.2.5)

(\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{U}},O_{E_{\nu}}})^{\wedge}\times_{\Spf O_{E_{\nu}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\simeq{J_{\mathrm{U}}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\big[\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{F_{v_{0}}}\times_{\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}}\Spf O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}\big)\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathrm{U}}\big],

which, by the transitivity of the action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on both sides of (
10.2.4
), is independent of the fiber. It remains to compare the Weil descent data down to
O_{E_{\nu}}
on both sides. The argument is identical to the corresponding point in §
9.2
: the Weil descent datum on the right hand side of (
10.2.3
) is given by multiplication by the special element
\widetilde{w}
on the second factor as in Corollary
6.2.3
; then one uses the transitive action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and the fact that the isomorphism (
10.2.5
) is independent of the fibers.

Finally, we vary the CM-types. We have to compare the descent data for two CM-types
\Phi_{0}^{+}
and
\Phi_{0}^{+^{\prime}}
containing
\varphi_{0}
. Again, the argument is identical to that in §
9.2
, so we omit it.
∎
